# Page type, continuity, specialty and encounter: the approach

Taxonomy **v29.2** (the version lives inside the files, as `version` /
`taxonomy_version`; no file or folder name carries a version). Written 2026-10-28;
replaces the 2026-10-25 version of this document.

This is the one document for how a chart's pages get their page type (L1 / L2 / L3),
how pages are grouped into documents (continuity), how each page's **Final** page
type, subtype (Codable / Non-Codable / Discharge) and specialty are decided from its
document, and how encounter type and page order follow. It describes what the code
does today, with the thresholds, weights and formulas taken from the code and the
canon files, and file:function references so every statement can be checked. The
canon the steps use is reproduced in the appendix, rendered from the JSON files.

**How the numbers in this document were obtained.** Every formula and threshold was
read from the code on 2026-10-28. The worked examples were *run through the real
code*: the keyword features, keyword scores, votes, page rules, confidences,
continuity gap scores, final-pass merges, Final page types and encounter decisions
below are the code's own output for the example pages. One thing could not be run
here: the page-axes and encounter models (the laptop this was written on has no
Python 3.12 runtime for torch). Where an example needs the model's probabilities,
they are **stated, invented inputs** (marked "model, assumed"), fed to the real
`axes.gate` / `classify_pages`; everything downstream of them is computed. Page text
in the examples is invented; no patient data appears in this document.

Status markers: **[built]** in the code; **[to build]** agreed design, not written;
**[decide]** waiting on a decision; **[fix]** a defect found while writing this
document (described, not changed: this document changes no code).

## What changed from the 2026-10-25 version

Every claim of the old document was checked against the code. Where they disagreed,
this edition follows the code. The differences:

| # | Old document said | The code does | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Progress Note L2 is decided by section tagging (`sections.json`): the dominant section is the L2 | No section tagging runs in the pipeline. The Progress Note L2 is the **keyword vote alone** (k = 1) over the fitted keyword weights; below 0.2 it is Others. `sections.json` is used only to label training pages (`training/taxonomy/rules/label_new_pages.py`) | `axes.distributions` drops the section L1's L2; `keyword_vote.vote` |
| 2 | Strong terms are "the `strong` lists in `class_keywords.json`"; adding words to the canon changes the vote | The vote reads **only `page_keyword_weights.json`**: a term (strong or not) added to `page_canon.json` / `class_keywords.json` has **no effect on the vote or on pins until `keyword_vote fit` is re-run**. It does take effect at once in `canon_entry`, `title_l1` and specialty-from-canon | `keyword_vote.scores`, `keyword_vote.pinned` |
| 3 | Canon defaults: strong 4, title 2.5 / 1, body 1, ambiguous 0.3 | Strong is 4.0 as a title and **3.0 in the body**; the rest as stated | `keyword_vote.DEFAULTS` |
| 4 | "Rules on top, after the vote" | Rules run after L1 **and L2** are picked and **before L3**; L3 is then voted under the rule's path | `axes.gate` |
| 5 | Continuity's final pass "splits two label dates and merges across a weak break" | It also runs **`progress_note_runs_on`**: a document opening on a Progress Note absorbs every following document until a label-read date (trust 5) differs, its last page is signed, the break is decisive, or a junk group follows. It does **not** check that the break is weak: breaks of +6 to +8 are merged (example in 4.10) | `engine._final_pass` |
| 6 | "different date of service (+5, decisive)" | Points = the **lower trust** of the two dates (5 label / admit / discharge, 3 collected / received / edge keyword / printed / unexplained, 2 keyless); decisive only at 5. Compared with the **open document's first dated page**, not the previous page | `engine.score_gap`, `continuity_canon.json → dos_trust` |
| 7 | "page type change (+3) or the same page type" | `page_type_change` = 3 × (1 − same) × conf(later), same = conf(later) when the L1s agree, else 1 − conf(later). It **also fires on same-L1 pairs** (+0.46 at confidence 0.81). `same_page_type` (−1) needs the whole name tuple to match | `engine.same_type_probability`, `score_gap` |
| 8 | — | `starts_mid_thought` (−1) fires on **every** gap where `wording_char` ≥ 0.05, because line text is lower-cased before the "starts with a lower-case letter" test. It makes the opening sub-score negative and so costs many clean breaks the 0.8 `conflict` penalty. **[fixed 2026-10-28]**: `Line.raw` keeps the line as read and the test runs on it | `engine.Line.raw`, `signals.starts_mid_thought` |
| 9 | The encounter's family is "its first page's L1, the document's Final type" | It is the first page's **Extracted** L1 (a page decided Others has none; then the next page with one). The Final page type is decided later, in `imaging_final` | `encounter_classify.encounter_family` |
| 10 | Each page's encounter vote is in `staging/continuity.json` | `continuity.json → encounters` holds the decision per encounter, **not the votes**; the votes, findings and negatives are written only to `debug/<chart>_encounter_evidence.csv` with `ENCOUNTER_DEBUG` on | `encounter/stage._stage_encounters` |
| 11 | ENC_03 (ambulatory surgery centre) open; "surgery center → F2F" waiting on the client | A negative now cancels inpatient evidence, tier 1 included, for "ambulatory surgery center" and "discharged home same day" — but **`applies_to.always` (Operative Note → Inpatient) is decided first**, so an ASC operative note is still Inpatient (example in 6.4) **[fix]** | `classify_pages`: `family_decision` before `first_page_decision` |
| 12 | Every Lab Report path except CT / MR angiography is Non-Codable | Also **`Lab Report > Pathology Report` (the L2 itself) is tagged codeable** while every L3 under it is non_codeable. A page whose Final path stops at that L2 (no L3) reads Codable **[decide]** | `page_taxonomy.json → codeability` |
| 13 | The family-wide codability rows are fixed in code | Only five families are: Progress Note and Operative Note (codeable), Consent and Authorization, Administrative, Demographics (non_codeable). Procedure Note and Discharge Summary come from the taxonomy tags | `taxonomy.L1_CODEABILITY` |
| 14 | — | `specialty_canon.json` says `inherit_across_document: true` (any page's specialty applies to the document) and lists `collapse_page_types`; **neither key is read**. The Final specialty is the document's first page's only | `imaging_final.encounter_specialties` |
| 15 | — | `utilities/merge_training_json.py` requires a `model_type` field, which v29.2 training records (L1 / L2 / L3 / path) do not have: it **drops every v29.2 record** (verified: two records in, "Dropped 2", 0 written). **[fixed 2026-10-28]**: a record needs `image`, `text` and `L1` or `model_type`; L1s are checked against the v29.2 `taxonomy.json` | `merge_training_json.REQUIRED`, `label_of` |
| 16 | "Should `staging/page_classification.json` become what later stages read first?" | It is written on every run and **read by nothing** (no stage, not review-ui; `read_staged` has no caller). Postgres `page_classification.axes` is what later stages read | `page_classify/stage.read_staged` |
| 17 | The canon has "about 295 canon page types" | `page_canon.json` has **348** entries (its `counts` block still says 295) | `page_canon.json` |
| 18 | `paths` are the page's best whole paths | They are, but the score multiplies the per-level votes and a level with no candidates counts 1.0, so a **shallow path can outrank the decided path** (in example A a canon-only lab page lists "Immunization" first; in example C a Progress Note page lists a Lab Report path first) | `axes._Vote.paths` |
| 19 | `continuity.json`'s `final.page_type` is the Final page type | It is continuity's carried value: the document's **first page's Extracted L1** (`document_finals`). The Final page type (packets, vote, placement) is only in `imaging_final` | `continuity/stage._write_scores` |
| 20 | — | `page_arbitration.json → axes.canon_min` / `l3_canon_min` are described as the canon's thresholds "where it answers alone"; `axes.canon_match`, their only reader, has **no caller**, so neither setting does anything | `axes.canon_match` |

Still as the old document said, verified: the encounter stage still runs **after**
continuity (`pipeline_stage` seq 90 vs 87), so "encounter Extracted before continuity"
is still **[to build]**; the continuity signals from the best paths, the encounter
change and the OCR-box layout are still **[to build]** (none is in
`engine.SIGNALS`); the client default (Outpatient Evisit) is not built; the
cross-encoder for sequencing is off and its ONNX model is not in the repository (only
its tokenizer is); titles below a letterhead (line 23 of the page) are still outside
the 12-line title zone.

## 1. The order of work

The stage order is the `pipeline_stage` table (`schema/v1.sql`). The stages this
document is about are in bold:

| seq | Stage (`stage_name`) | Reads | Writes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10 | `ocr_quality` | page image | rotation, handwriting (`printed_or_handwritten`, `hw_confidence`), page tag (`document_type`) |
| 20 | `ocr_prelim` | page image | Tesseract text |
| 30 | `blank_junk` pass 1 | Tesseract text | exact duplicates, printed blank / junk |
| 40 | `ocr_final1` | page image | Docling / RapidOCR text + word polygons (`ocr/<chart>_final1.json`) |
| 50 | `ocr_final2` | page image | Azure Document Intelligence text + line polygons (`ocr/<chart>_final2.json`) |
| 55 | `section_headers` | Final 1 / Final 2 JSON | `section_headers`, `section_header_candidates` in those JSONs |
| 56 | `kv_extract` | best text | `staging/extraction.json`: DOS rows with their tier, page numbers, e-signatures, visit IDs, headings |
| 60 | `blank_junk` pass 2 | best text | final Main / Blank / Junk / Duplicate verdict |
| 70 | `member_verify` | best text | member rows |
| 75 | **`page_subtype`** | best text, OCR headings | **Extracted L1 / L2 / L3, specialty, codeability** (`page_classification`, `staging/page_classification.json`) |
| 80 | `dos_extract` | text, Extracted subtype | each page's DOS |
| 87 | **`continuity`** | everything above | **documents** (`page_continuity_results`, `staging/continuity.json`, `continuity_detail.json`) |
| 90 | **`encounter_type`** | Extracted L1, text, documents, encounter model | encounter per document (`encounter_type_results`; `encounters` in `continuity.json`) |
| 95 | `page_sequencing` | text, page numbers, documents | suggested reading order |
| 100 | **`imaging_final`** | every table above | **Final page type, subtype (codability), specialty**, DOS, encounter, confidences (`imaging_final`, `imaging/<chart>_final.csv`) |

```
 per page (Extracted: the page alone)              per document (Final: the page in context)
 ─────────────────────────────────────            ──────────────────────────────────────────
  OCR → blank/junk → key/value → member
  PAGE TYPE  L1 / L2 / L3  (page_subtype) ──┐
     model + keyword vote, page rules       │
     specialty, Extracted codeability       ├──▶ CONTINUITY (documents: gap scores,
  DATE OF SERVICE (dos_extract) ────────────┘        final pass)
                                                        │
                                                        ▼
                                                 ENCOUNTER TYPE (per document)
                                                 PAGE SEQUENCING
                                                        │
                                                        ▼
                                                 IMAGING_FINAL: Final page type,
                                                 subtype / codability, specialty,
                                                 DOS, encounter; confidences
```

Continuity never reads a Final value, so a Final value cannot feed back into the
grouping (`imaging_final/stage.run`, comment). **[to build]** The page's own
encounter vote (Extracted) should move before continuity so a change of setting can
count toward a break (4.12); today the encounter stage runs after continuity and does
both jobs.

## 2. The taxonomy and the two answers per page

**One taxonomy everywhere.** `core-pipeline/stages/lib/keyword-canon/page_taxonomy.json`
(pipeline), `training/bert-training/taxonomy.json` (trainer) and
`training/taxonomy/taxonomy.json` (labelling) carry the same v29.2 label space. The
model folder's `label_space.json` must say `taxonomy_version: v29.2`
(`page_arbitration.json → axes.taxonomy_version`), else the model is refused at load
and the keywords decide (`axes.check_taxonomy`; `/health → page_axes_model`).

| Level | Values | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **L1** | 17: Progress Note, Lab Report, Operative Note, Procedure Note, Discharge Summary, Clinical Summary, Consent and Authorization, Demographics, Immunization, Medication Record, Order and Requisition, Problem List, Forms and Questionnaires, Provider Letter, Patient Communication, Administrative, Others | the model has 16 (Others is its folded `OTHER` class) |
| **L2** | listed per L1 (appendix A.3); Others last in each | under **Progress Note** the L2 is the *content section* (CC/HPI, Visit Diagnosis, Assessment and Plan, Summary, Problem List, Medication List, Lab and Imaging Results, Procedure List, Vitals, Physical Exam, Questionnaire, Patient Instructions, Signature, Others) |
| **L3** | only under the four Lab Report L2s: Lab Result (11), Radiology Report, Pathology Report, Diagnostic Study | `label_space.L3` keys; `axes.l3_names` |
| **Specialty** | 34 in the taxonomy (incl. Others, Unknown) + **General**, the pipeline's fallback | the pipeline never writes Unknown |
| **Codeability** | codeable, non_codeable, discharge (needs_review exists in code; no path carries it) | from the taxonomy tag of the path; 5 families fixed in code |

**Two answers per page, both kept.**

| | Decided from | Changes with neighbours? | Stored in | Example |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Extracted** | the page alone | never | `page_classification` (+ `axes` JSONB), `staging/page_classification.json`, `imaging/<chart>_codeable.csv` | a page that is wholly a CMP result → Lab Report (Lab Result) / Chemistry Report |
| **Final** | the document the page sits in | yes | `imaging_final`, `imaging/<chart>_final.csv` | that page inside an office-visit document → Progress Note / Lab and Imaging Results, Codable |

**The page-essence rule (labels, and therefore Extracted).** A page is labelled by what
the page itself holds, because the model sees one page at a time. A page that is
wholly lab results, imaging results, a medication list or an order list carries that
class (Lab Report / Medication Record / Order and Requisition) even inside an office
note; headers, footers, a signature block, an allergy list or a short problem list do
not change that. Only a split page, one that also carries note content (HPI, interval
history, ROS, exam, assessment, plan), is Progress Note. Putting pages back into their
documents is the Final step's job (section 5).

**How a page type is written** (`axes.page_names`): with L1 only, `page_type` = L1 and
`page_subtype` = Others (an L1 is never its own subtype); with L1 and L2, L1 / L2; with
three levels, `"L1 (L2)"` / L3. `model_type` is the path `L1 > L2 > L3`.
`arbitration.path_of` reads them back.

---
## 3. L1 / L2 / L3: the Extracted page type, page alone

Stage `page_subtype` (`core-pipeline/stages/lib/page_classify/stage.py: run`), one page
at a time, for every page that is not blank, junk or duplicate. The decision is
`axes.predict` → `axes.gate`.

```
 page text (Final2 → Final1 → Tesseract)      OCR heading lines (Final2 / Final1 JSON)
        │                                               │
        ├──▶ page-axes model (Bio_ClinicalBERT, 512 tokens) ──▶ p(L1), p(L2|L1), p(L3|L2), p(specialty)
        └──▶ keyword canon: terms on the page → features "term|title" / "term|body"
                     │                               └──▶ weights per class per level (fitted)
                     ▼
   L1:  one strong title term pins a class?  ──yes──▶ that class
        else vote = (1−k)·p_model + k·kw/Σkw ; best ≥ 0.20 ? best : Others
   L2 (under the L1): the same; under Progress Note the keywords alone (k = 1)
   page rules (clinical_summary, hospital_stay, lab_guard) may re-place L1 / L2
   L3 (under L1 > L2, only the 4 Lab Report L2s): the same vote; below 0.20 → Others
                     │
                     ▼
   + every other L1's best L2 (under_l1) + the vote's 5 best paths + the model's 5 best paths
   + the canon page type behind the path (codeability), specialty, confidences
```

### 3.1 Inputs

| Input | Where it comes from | Code |
|---|---|---|
| **Page text** | the best OCR the page has: Azure (Final 2), else Docling / RapidOCR (Final 1), else Tesseract — but **never Tesseract for a handwritten, uncertain, mixed or low-quality page** (the page then has no text and gets no page type: `decided_by = no_text`) | `stages/_support.best_page_text`, `db.page_blocks_prelim` |
| **Heading lines** | the OCR layout's `section_header_candidates` and `section_headers` from the Final 2 JSON, else Final 1 | `page_classify/stage._headings` |
| **The page-axes model** | `core-pipeline/models/page-family/page-axes/` (`encoder/`, `heads.pt`, `label_space.json`): Bio_ClinicalBERT, one encoder, hierarchical heads (L2 masked to the L1's L2s, L3 to the L2's L3s) + a specialty head; text cut to 512 tokens, **85% from the start, 15% from the end** (`head_fraction` 0.85) | `axes._load`, `axes_model.encode_texts`, `axes_model.decode` |
| Model answer used | `distributions`: p(L1) over 16 L1s (+ `OTHER`), p(L2 \| L1) under every L1, p(L3 \| L2) under every L3 parent; `L1_top`; `specialty_top`; `paths` | `axes_model.distributions`, `top_paths` |
| **Keyword canon** | `keyword-canon/page_canon.json`: 348 canon page types, each with a path in v29.2 names, a codeability tag, a specialty and a lexicon of `strong_terms`, `title_terms`, `body_terms`, `ambiguous_terms` (2,929 terms); `class_keywords.json`'s curated words are merged into it by `page_classify/class_keywords.py` | `keywords.build` |
| **Keyword weights** | `keyword-canon/page_keyword_weights.json`: per level, feature → class → weight, fitted on the labelled training pages; plus the vote's settings and the strong-term table | `keyword_vote.weights`, `settings` |
| **Page rules** | `page_arbitration.json → axes_rules` | `page_rules.apply` |
| **Specialty lexicon** | `keyword-canon/specialty_canon.json` | `specialty_keywords` |

### 3.2 Keyword matching: which canon terms are on the page

`keywords.matches` (`page_canon.json → how_to_match`, `title_zone_lines` 12):

1. **Whole words or phrases, case-insensitive.** A term `a b` is the regex
   `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])a\s+b(?![A-Za-z0-9])`, so "lab" never matches inside "label" and a
   phrase may wrap over spaces.
2. **Longest match wins.** A term inside a longer term that matched at the same place
   counts only as the longer one ("history" inside "history of present illness").
3. **Where it counts.** A `strong` or `title` term counts as **title** when the line it
   sits on is used as a heading *and* the match starts within the first 12 non-empty
   lines (the title zone). A line is a heading when (`keywords.is_heading`): the line
   is exactly the term, or it is one of the OCR's detected heading lines, or — for a
   term of two or more words — the line starts with the term and is at most 25
   characters longer ("OFFICE VISIT 03/21/2024"). A one-word term is a title only as a
   line of its own or a detected heading. A title term used as a heading below the
   zone counts as **heading**; anywhere else (inside a sentence) it counts as **body**.
   Body and ambiguous terms always count as body.
4. **Features for the vote.** Each distinct term becomes the feature `"term|title"`
   (where = title) or `"term|body"` (everything else, a heading below the zone
   included): `keyword_vote.features`.

So "SUPERBILL" alone on line 1 gives `superbill|title`; "superbill" inside a sentence
gives `superbill|body`; "Office Visit 03/21/2024" on line 4 gives `office visit|title`.

### 3.3 Keyword weights: how much a term says about each class

A feature is not a vote for one class: it carries a **weight for every class, at every
level**, learned from the labelled pages (`keyword_vote.fit`, `_learn`):

```
prior(c)       = (pages of c + 0.5) / (N + 0.5 · classes)
P(c | f)       = (pages of c carrying f + 2 · prior(c)) / (pages carrying f + 2)     PRIOR_STRENGTH = 2
learned(f, c)  = max(0, ln(P(c | f) / prior(c)))
blend          = n_f / (n_f + 5)                                                    SHRINK = 5
weight(f, c)   = min(6, blend · learned + (1 − blend) · default(f, c))              MAX_WEIGHT = 6
               (stored only when ≥ 0.05)
```

`default(f, c)` is the canon tier the term is listed under, for the class of the entry
that lists it (`keyword_vote.DEFAULTS`):

| Tier | as title | in the body |
|---|---|---|
| strong | 4.0 | 3.0 |
| title | 2.5 | 1.0 |
| body | 1.0 | 1.0 |
| ambiguous | 0.3 | 0.3 |

So a term seen on many pages is judged by the pages (a term every page carries weighs
about 0; one only consent forms carry weighs up to 6), and a term the pages never show
keeps its canon default. Separate tables are fitted per level: L1; L2 under each L1;
L3 under each of the four Lab Report L2s. Today's file was fitted on the
Bio_ClinicalBERT run's split (train 2,005 / validation 382 / test 397 pages) and holds
4,278 L1 features.

**Strong terms pin.** `fit` also writes `strong`: for every canon `strong_terms` entry,
`"term|title"` → the L1 (and L2, L3) of each entry listing it (115 L1 features today:
`superbill`, `echocardiogram`, `authorization`, `audiogram`, ...).

> **Important.** Both the weights and the strong table are produced by `fit`. A term
> added to `page_canon.json` or `class_keywords.json` has **no weight and pins
> nothing until `python -m stages.lib.page_classify.keyword_vote fit --data
> training_data.jsonl --split split.csv` is re-run** (9.3).

The second, older weighting in `page_canon.json → weights` (title in zone 3, title
elsewhere 2, body 1, ambiguous 0.5; ambiguous only after another term of the same
entry) is used **only** by `keywords.classify`: to pick the canon page type behind a
decided path (3.8), the page's `title_l1`, and the canon's own confidence
(`arbitration.keyword_confidence`: title hit 0.6, body 0.3, + 0.05 per point of margin,
capped 0.9 / 0.6). It does not vote.

### 3.4 The vote, level by level

`axes._Vote.pick`, `keyword_vote.vote`. At L1, then L2 under the winning L1, then L3
under the winning L2:

```
kw(c)  = Σ over the page's features of weight(feature, c)       (keyword_vote.scores)
K      = Σ kw(c) over the level's classes (positive scores only)
k      = share[level] · min(1, K / full[level])     with a model
k      = 1                                          without a model (or no model answer at this level)
vote(c)= (1 − k) · p_model(c) + k · kw(c) / K       (keyword term 0 when K = 0)
```

1. **Pin first.** If exactly one class at this level is pinned by a strong title
   feature (`keyword_vote.pinned`), it wins outright (`who = canon`). Two or more pins
   cancel and the vote decides.
2. **Else the vote.** The best class wins when `vote ≥ min[level]` and > 0
   (`who = model` when it is the model's own favourite, else `canon`).
3. **Else nothing:** L1 → **Others** (`decided_by = others`); L2 → none (written as
   "L1 / Others"); L3 → **Others**.

The settings live in `page_keyword_weights.json → settings` (tuned on the validation
pages by `keyword_vote evaluate --save`, 2026-10-25; `min` set by the user):

| | L1 | L2 | L3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `share` (keywords' part at full evidence) | 0.2 | 0.5 | 0.2 |
| `full` (keyword score that counts as full evidence) | 6 | 1 | 6 |
| `min` (below: Others) | 0.2 | 0.2 | 0.2 |

What this means in practice:

* **At L1 the model leads**: the keywords can move at most 20% of the vote. At L2 they
  move up to half, and reach "full evidence" with a score of only 1.
* **Thin evidence cannot swing a page**: k grows with K. One ambiguous word (0.3) at L1
  gives k = 0.2 × 0.3 / 6 = 0.01.
* **Progress Note's L2 is the keywords' alone.** The model never learned it (its L2
  head leaves the section L1 out: `axes.distributions` drops it), so there k = 1 and
  the vote is each section's share of the keyword score. Because a note page carries
  words of many sections (HPI, exam, vitals, plan), the best share is often under 0.2
  and the subtype is Others (example C). There is **no section tagging** in the
  pipeline (see "What changed", row 1).
* **Without a model** (no folder, no torch, or a model of another taxonomy) every level
  is the keywords' alone (k = 1) and `route` is null: visible in the data and in
  `/health`.
* **Without a canon term** the model votes alone (k = 0).

`route` (`axes.route`: model when its best L1 ≥ 0.40 with a lead ≥ 0.10, canon when it
is `OTHER` or ≥ 0.25, else review) is **reported only**; it decides nothing.

### 3.5 Page rules: logic a word list cannot say

`page_rules.apply`, configured in `page_arbitration.json → axes_rules`, after L1 and L2
are picked and **before L3**. The first rule that fires sets L1 and L2 (sources
`rule`, `rules` names it); L3 is then voted under the new path. Titles are read in the
title zone: the first `title_zone_lines` (12) non-empty lines plus the OCR's detected
headings, normalised; a title counts when a line **starts with** it (whole words).

| Rule | Fires when | Gives | Codeability if the canon has no entry |
|---|---|---|---|
| `clinical_summary` | L1 is not Discharge Summary, and a title-zone line starts with a Summary-of-Care title (Continuity of Care Document, Summary of Care, Patient Summary of Care, Transition of Care Document / Summary, C-CDA, CCD) or carries a CDA OID (`2.16.840.1.113883`, `1.2.840.114350`) | Clinical Summary > Summary of Care (C-CDA) | non_codeable |
| | ... or starts with After Visit Summary, After-Visit Summary, Patient Clinical Summary | Clinical Summary > After-Visit Summary | |
| `hospital_stay` | L1 is Progress Note, the page carries stay evidence (admission / admit date, date of admission, admitting physician / provider / diagnosis, room / bed, room # / bed no, observation status / unit, inpatient status / admission / unit / stay, hospital day) **and** a title-zone line starts with an H&P title (History and Physical, History & Physical, H&P, H & P, Admission History, Admission H&P, Admission Note) | Discharge Summary > History and Physical | discharge |
| | ... or a consultation title (Consultation Note(s), Consultation Report, Consult Note, Inpatient Consultation, Inpatient Consult) | Discharge Summary > Consultation Note | |
| `lab_guard` | L1 is Progress Note, **no** note-body pattern (chief complaint, history of present illness, `\bhpi\b`, interval history, review of systems, `\bros\s*:`, physical exam, assessment (and/&)? plan, `assessment\s*:`, impression and plan, `\bplan\s*:`, `subjective\s*:`, `objective\s*:`), and **≥ 2** lab markers (specimen, accession, performing lab / laboratory / site, result status, reference range, ref range, collected, collection date, reported, ordering provider / physician, final report, flag, units, lab director, CLIA) **or** a panel title on one of the first 3 lines (`^(cbc|cmp|bmp|lipid panel|a1c|urinalysis|tsh|culture|surgical pathology)\b.*\b(final|result|results|panel|report)\b`) | Lab Report > Lab Result | non_codeable |

The hospital-stay rule is one-way: a page the vote already made Discharge Summary >
H&P stays so without evidence on the page (the evidence may be on another page of
the admission). `"enabled": false` turns a rule off.

### 3.6 L3

Only the four Lab Report L2s have named L3s (`axes.l3_names`; label space
`L3["Lab Report > Lab Result"]` etc.). For those, L3 is voted with the same formula
(share 0.2, full 6, min 0.2) under `"L1 > L2"`; below the minimum L3 is **Others**
(`sources[2] = others`). Under any other L2 there is no L3.

### 3.7 Alternatives kept: `under_l1`, `paths`, `model_paths`

* **`under_l1`** (`axes.gate`): for **every other L1** that has L2s, the L2 (and L3) the
  page would take there, by the same `pick` (pins, vote, minimum):
  `{L1: {l2, l2_confidence, l2_source, l3, l3_confidence}}`. The Final step reads it when
  the page's document has another L1 (5.4). An L1 whose best L2 does not reach 0.2 is
  absent.
* **`paths`** (`_Vote.paths`): the vote's best whole paths: for the 3 best L1s, their 3
  best L2s and best L3, score = vote(L1) × vote(L2 | L1) × vote(L3 | L2), at most 3
  under one L1, the best 5 kept. A level with no candidate counts 1.0 — so **a path
  that stops early can outrank a deeper one** (an L1 with no L2 evidence keeps its whole
  L1 vote). Read `paths` as "what else the page could be", not as a ranking to take the
  first of: the decided path is in `l1` / `l2` / `l3`.
* **`model_paths`**: the model's own best 5 paths (p(L1)·p(L2|L1)·p(L3|L2), at most 3
  per L1), `folded: true` for the model's `OTHER`.

### 3.8 The canon entry behind the path, and Extracted codeability

`axes.canon_entry`: among the canon entries under the decided L1 [> L2 [> L3]], the best
by `keywords.classify`; with no keyword hit, the entry named after the deepest level,
else any entry of exactly that path when they all share one codeability tag. This is
`canon_page_type` (also used by encounter tier 1 and the DOS default-date types through
`axes.names_of`).

Extracted codeability (`axes.gate`), first that applies:

1. the entry's tag when its rule is `page_type_tag` and the tag is not needs_review;
2. a page rule's own codeability when the canon has no entry under the rule's path;
3. **overriding both**: the family tag of `taxonomy.L1_CODEABILITY` (Progress Note,
   Operative Note → codeable; Consent and Authorization, Administrative, Demographics
   → non_codeable);
4. else needs_review.

This is the page's own view. **The codability the client gets is the Final one**
(5.6), from the Final path.

### 3.9 Extracted specialty

`axes.gate`, first that applies (never Unknown):

| # | Source | Condition | `sources[3]` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the model's best specialty | not Unknown / Others; p ≥ `specialty_min` **0.50**; lead over the second ≥ `specialty_lead` **0.10**; trained on ≥ `specialty_min_train_pages` **10** pages (`label_space.json → train_pages.specialty`) | model |
| 2 | the specialty keywords (`specialty_keywords.specialty`) | a `header` term in the top **18%** of the page's non-empty lines scores **6**; a header term lower down or a `body` term anywhere scores **1**; each term once; best ≥ **6** with a lead ≥ **3** | canon |
| 3 | the canon entry's specialty (Sleep Study Report → Sleep Study) | the entry has one, not Unknown / Others | canon |
| 4 | **General** | — | others |

Of 29 trained specialties, Nutrition (3 training pages), Wound Care (5) and Nursing (7)
never pass rule 1 and are left to the keywords. A practice name in the letterhead
("Lakeside Family Medicine" → Primary Care's header term "family medicine") is
usually what decides rule 2. The provider's credentials (MD, DPM, OD ...) and the
provider name are **not** used for specialty today (10, suggestion S12).

### 3.10 Confidence of each level

`axes._Vote.confidence`, `stages/lib/confidence.py` (section 8):

```
backed(p, s) = p + (1 − p) · 0.5 · s                       (agree_lift 0.5)
strength     = min(1, K / full[level])                     support of the top keyword class = kw(c)/K · strength
```

| Situation | Level confidence |
|---|---|
| the vote's winner is also the keywords' top class | backed(p_model(c), kw(c)/K · strength) |
| keywords' top class is another and strength ≥ 0.5 | p_model(c) × 0.7 (`keywords_disagree`) |
| pinned by a strong title | backed(p_model(c), 1.0) |
| set by a page rule | backed(p_model(c), 1.0), × 0.7 when the model's own favourite is another class |
| no model | kw(c)/K · strength (1.0 when pinned or a rule) |
| Others (L1) | the model's p(OTHER) × 0.6 (`fallback`); without a model 0.5 × 0.6 |
| Others (L3) | (1 − best p_model) × 0.6 |

* **Page type confidence** (`axes.page_type_confidence`, stored in
  `page_classification.confidence`): the product of the named levels' confidences
  **except the deepest** (the subtype keeps its own and does not flag the page;
  2026-10-25, user); × 0.85 (`no_model`) once when decided without a model; then × 0.9
  for a handwritten / mixed page and × 0.85 for under 150 characters
  (`conf.page_reasons`). Below **0.50** the page needs review.
* **Subtype confidence**: the deepest named level's own.
* **Codeability confidence**: the L1's (the family settles codeability).
* **Specialty confidence** (`axes.specialty_confidence`): the model's p, backed by the
  keyword score (`min(1, score / 12)`) when the keywords name the same specialty,
  × 0.7 when they name another; General: (1 − the best named p) × 0.6.

### 3.11 Others and every fallback

| What | When | Result |
|---|---|---|
| L1 Others | no class pinned and the best vote < 0.20 | `page_type` Others, `page_subtype` Others, `decided_by` others, `needs_review` true; no L2 / L3; keeps `paths` and `under_l1` for the Final step |
| L2 none | the best L2 vote < 0.20 (common under Progress Note) | written "L1 / Others" |
| L3 Others | the best L3 vote < 0.20 | "L1 (L2) / Others" |
| no text | `best_page_text` empty (e.g. a handwritten page with no Final OCR) | `page_type` Unknown, `decided_by` no_text, review |
| no model | model folder missing / unloadable / other taxonomy | keywords alone at every level, confidence × 0.85 |
| specialty | nothing passes | General |

A page left Others or Unknown takes its document's L1 in the Final step (5.4).

### 3.12 What is written

Per page, `page_classification` (`db.upsert_page_classification`): `page_type`,
`page_subtype`, `model_type`, `classification_category` (codeable / non_codeable /
discharge_summary), `confidence`, `decided_by` (model / canon / rule / others /
blank_junk / `duplicate_of:<page id>` / no_text), `needs_review` (no page type, a
needs_review tag, confidence < 0.50, or decided Others), and `axes`, the whole answer
(`AxesResult.as_json`). The same goes to `staging/page_classification.json` (9.5) and
`imaging/<chart>_codeable.csv` (frozen CSV header, then the `axes_*` columns).

Blank and junk pages get no page type: the junk label is the subtype (Blank, Fax Cover,
Invoice ...), `non_codeable`. A duplicate takes its original's page type, subtype and
axes (followed through a chain of duplicates, `stage._original_of`) and stays
non_codeable (one copy is coded).

### 3.13 Worked examples

Each example's keyword features, scores, votes, rules and confidences are the real
code's output (`axes.gate` on the page text). Model probabilities, where used, are
assumed inputs.

#### Example A — a lab page, the model sure

```
NORTHFIELD REGIONAL LABORATORY   CLIA 00D0000000
Patient: SAMPLE, PAT   DOB 02/03/1950   Ordering Provider: J. Doe, MD
Accession: NR-24-10293   Collected: 03/14/2024 08:10   Reported: 03/14/2024 13:45
COMPREHENSIVE METABOLIC PANEL - FINAL REPORT
Test             Result   Flag   Units     Reference Range
Sodium           138             mmol/L    135-145
Potassium        5.4      H      mmol/L    3.5-5.1
...  Glucose 182 H, BUN 28 H, Creatinine 1.6 H, eGFR 42 L, Calcium 9.1
Result status: Final   Performing lab: Northfield Regional Laboratory
Lab Director: A. Example, MD
```

**Step 1, features** (21, all body: no title term heads a line): accession, bun,
calcium, chloride, clia, collected, creatinine, dob, egfr, flag, glucose, laboratory,
lab, md, panel, potassium, reference range, reported, result, sodium, units.

**Step 2, keyword score per L1** (sum of each feature's fitted weight for the class):

| L1 | kw | biggest contributions |
|---|---|---|
| Lab Report | 15.73 | accession 1.38, collected 1.23, clia 1.22, reference range 1.15, egfr 1.14, glucose 1.06 ... |
| Immunization | 6.42 | flag 2.45, reference range 1.33, laboratory 0.93 |
| Medication Record | 6.25 | units 1.70, sodium 1.42 |
| Order and Requisition | 4.72 | laboratory 1.50 |
| all L1s (K) | 53.04 | |

Note how the learned weights carry small-sample habits: "flag" weighs 2.45 for
Immunization and "sodium" 1.42 for Medication Record. Spread over 21 features they do
little; that is what k's cap is for.

**Step 3, the L1 vote.** k = 0.2 × min(1, 53.04 / 6) = 0.2. Model (assumed): Lab Report
0.86, Progress Note 0.08, Order and Requisition 0.03.

| L1 | p_model | kw / K | vote = 0.8·p + 0.2·kw/K |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Lab Report** | 0.86 | 0.297 | 0.688 + 0.059 = **0.747** |
| Progress Note | 0.08 | 0.014 | 0.067 |
| Order and Requisition | 0.03 | 0.089 | 0.042 |

No pin; 0.747 ≥ 0.2 → **Lab Report** (`who` model).

**Step 4, L2 under Lab Report.** K = 24.16, k = 0.5 × min(1, 24.16 / 1) = 0.5. Model
(assumed): Lab Result 0.90, Pathology 0.06.

| L2 | p | kw | vote |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Lab Result** | 0.90 | 15.03 | 0.45 + 0.5 × 15.03/24.16 = **0.761** |
| Pathology Report | 0.06 | 7.83 | 0.192 |

**Step 5, page rules.** L1 is not Progress Note: no rule applies.

**Step 6, L3 under Lab Report > Lab Result.** K = 32.5, k = 0.2. Model (assumed):
Chemistry 0.62, Hematology 0.14, Glucose 0.10. Chemistry 0.496 + 0.2 × 9.85/32.5 =
**0.557**; Hematology 0.121; Glucose Report 0.102 → **Chemistry Report**.

**Step 7, confidences.**
L1 = backed(0.86, 15.73/53.04 × 1) = 0.86 + 0.14 × 0.5 × 0.297 = **0.881**;
L2 = backed(0.90, 0.622) = **0.931**; L3 = backed(0.62, 0.303) = **0.678**.
Page type = L1 × L2 (the deepest, L3, left out) = **0.820** (good).

**Result.** `page_type` "Lab Report (Lab Result)", `page_subtype` "Chemistry Report",
`canon_page_type` Chemistry Report, Extracted codeability non_codeable (0.881),
specialty **General** (0.546: the model's best is Unknown, so rule 1 fails; no
specialty keyword; the entry has none; (1 − 0.09) × 0.6). `paths`: Lab Report > Lab
Result > Chemistry Report 0.317, Lab Report > Pathology Report > Cytometry Report
0.042, Order and Requisition > Laboratory Requisition 0.028, Progress Note > Lab and
Imaging Results 0.015, ... `under_l1["Progress Note"]` = Lab and Imaging Results.

**The same page with no model** (keywords alone, k = 1): L1 Lab Report at 15.73 / 53.04
= **0.297** (just above 0.2), L2 Lab Result 0.622, L3 Chemistry Report 0.303; page type
0.297 × 0.622 × 0.85 (`no_model`) = **0.157 → review**. And `paths[0]` is
"Immunization" (0.121): Immunization has no L2 evidence, so its path keeps its whole
L1 vote, while the decided path is 0.297 × 0.622 × 0.303 = 0.056 (3.7).

#### Example B — the same lab page, the model saying Progress Note: the lab guard

Model (assumed): Progress Note 0.55, Lab Report 0.40.

| L1 | p | kw / K | vote |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Progress Note** | 0.55 | 0.014 | 0.440 + 0.003 = **0.443** |
| Lab Report | 0.40 | 0.297 | 0.320 + 0.059 = 0.379 |

The vote says Progress Note. Then `lab_guard`: L1 is Progress Note; none of the
note-body patterns is on the page; lab markers found: accession, performing lab,
result status, reference range, collected, reported, ordering provider, final report,
flag, units, lab director, CLIA (12 ≥ 2) → **Lab Report > Lab Result**, then L3
Chemistry Report as before.

Confidences: L1 = backed(0.40, 1.0) = 0.70, × 0.7 because the model's favourite was
Progress Note = **0.49**; L2 = backed(0.90, 1.0) = 0.95; page type 0.49 × 0.95 =
**0.466 → needs review**. `sources` "rule/rule/model/others/canon", `rules`
["lab_guard"]. The guard protects the client's main worry (a lab report read as a
note), and the low confidence tells the reviewer the model disagreed.

#### Example C — an office note: why the Progress Note subtype is often Others

```
Lakeside Family Medicine
1200 Example Road, Suite 4   Phone (555) 010-0000
Patient: SAMPLE, PAT   DOB 02/03/1950
OFFICE VISIT  03/21/2024
Provider: R. Placeholder, MD
Chief Complaint: follow up of diabetes and kidney function
History of Present Illness: 74 year old seen for follow up of type 2 diabetes. Labs on
03/14 show creatinine 1.6 and potassium 5.4. Reports fatigue, denies chest pain.
Review of Systems: negative except as in HPI.
Vitals: BP 142/86  HR 78  Wt 92 kg
Physical Exam: alert, no edema, lungs clear.
Assessment and Plan: 1. Type 2 diabetes with CKD stage 3b ... 2. Hyperkalemia ...
Electronically signed by R. Placeholder, MD 03/21/2024 16:02
```

Features: 25, one of them a title: `office visit|title` (line 4 starts with the
two-word title term and is within 25 characters of it). L1 keyword scores: Progress
Note 12.35, Discharge Summary 5.88, Lab Report 4.19, Order and Requisition 4.07; K =
51.12.

L1 vote with the model (assumed Progress Note 0.62, Lab Report 0.30): Progress Note
0.496 + 0.2 × 12.35/51.12 = **0.544**; Lab Report 0.240 + 0.016 = 0.256 →
**Progress Note**, confidence backed(0.62, 0.242) = **0.666**.

L2 under Progress Note, keywords alone (k = 1), K = 52.77:

| Progress Note L2 | kw | vote = kw / K |
|---|---|---|
| Physical Exam | 8.43 | 0.160 |
| CC/HPI | 8.24 | 0.156 |
| Lab and Imaging Results | 6.96 | 0.132 |
| Vitals | 4.85 | 0.092 |
| Medication List | 4.26 | 0.081 |
| Assessment and Plan | 3.89 | 0.074 |

No section reaches 0.2 → no L2: **"Progress Note / Others"**. A complete visit note
names every section, so the keyword share spreads and the subtype stays Others; a page
that is mostly one section (a page of only the exam) can reach 0.2. Codeability
codeable (family). Specialty: the model (assumed Primary Care 0.58, Nephrology 0.21)
passes the gate; the keywords agree ("family medicine" in the top 18% of lines: 6) →
**Primary Care**, backed(0.58, 6/12) = **0.685**. The lab guard does not apply: the
page has note-body sections.

`paths` here starts with "Lab Report > Lab Result > Chemistry Report 0.103" ahead of
"Progress Note > Physical Exam 0.087": the Lab Report path is led by the model at
every level while the Progress Note L2 is the diffuse keyword share (3.7).

#### Example D — a superbill: a strong title beats a confident model

```
SUPERBILL
Lakeside Family Medicine   Tax ID 00-0000000
Patient: SAMPLE, PAT   Account 55120   Date of Service 03/21/2024
CPT 99214 Office visit, established, moderate $180.00
CPT 83036 Hemoglobin A1c $25.00
ICD-10 E11.22 N18.32 E87.5   Copay collected $20.00
```

Features include `superbill|title` (the line is exactly the term). The strong table
pins **Administrative** at L1 and **Superbill** at L2 under Administrative. The model
(assumed) says Progress Note 0.70, Administrative 0.18; the vote would have given
Progress Note 0.568 against Administrative 0.215, but **one pin wins outright**.

Confidences: L1 = backed(0.18, 1.0) = **0.59**; L2 pinned with no model distribution
under Administrative = 1.0; page type **0.59** (passable). Without a model: 1.0 at
both levels, page type 1.0 × 0.85 = **0.85**. Codeability non_codeable (Administrative
is a fixed family).

#### Example E — an admission H&P: the hospital-stay rule

```
History and Physical
Mercy Example Hospital   Room/Bed: 412-B
Admission Date: 05/02/2024   Admitting Physician: K. Sample, MD
Chief Complaint: shortness of breath
History of Present Illness: 68 year old admitted from the emergency department ...
Assessment and Plan: acute on chronic systolic heart failure - IV furosemide,
telemetry, echocardiogram, cardiology consult.
```

L1 keyword scores: Discharge Summary 12.89, Operative Note 7.46, Order and
Requisition 6.41, Problem List 6.26, Progress Note 5.78 (K = 65.2). Model (assumed):
Progress Note 0.58, Discharge Summary 0.35. Vote: Progress Note 0.464 + 0.018 =
**0.482**; Discharge Summary 0.280 + 0.040 = 0.320 → Progress Note.

`hospital_stay`: L1 is Progress Note; stay evidence "Admission Date", "Admitting
Physician", "Room/Bed"; title zone line 1 starts with "History and Physical" →
**Discharge Summary > History and Physical**.

Confidences: L1 = backed(0.35, 1.0) = 0.675 × 0.7 (model preferred Progress Note) =
**0.4725**; L2 = backed(p(H&P | Discharge Summary) 0.55, 1.0) = 0.775; page type 0.4725
→ **review**. Codeability **discharge** (taxonomy tag of the path). Specialty: model
Cardiology 0.66 (lead 0.52, 129 training pages) → **Cardiology** 0.66 (the specialty
keywords score Cardiology only 4: "cardiology" and "telemetry" are body terms, and the
best needs 6).

The same page with no model reads **Others** (Discharge Summary 12.89 / 65.2 = 0.198,
just under 0.2): the rule cannot fire because it needs the vote to say Progress Note
first. Final then gives the page its document's L1 (5.4).

#### Example F — a consent, keywords alone

```
AUTHORIZATION FOR RELEASE OF PROTECTED HEALTH INFORMATION
I authorize Lakeside Family Medicine to disclose my health information ...
This authorization expires one year from the date signed. I may revoke ...
Signing is voluntary ...   Patient or legal guardian signature ___  Witness ___
```

No title feature ("authorization" is a strong one-word term, but the line is longer
than the term, so it counts as body). L1 keyword scores: Consent and Authorization
28.39 (authorize 2.53, authorization 2.46, disclosure 2.45, disclose 2.38, guardian 2.20,
revoke 2.12 ...), Demographics 7.71; with no model, vote = 28.39 / 64.75 = **0.438** →
Consent and Authorization. L2: Release of Information 6.13, Consent for Treatment 5.84,
HIPAA and Privacy 4.79 (K 25.3) → 0.242 → **Release of Information** (the two leaders
are close: a reviewer should look). Page type 0.438 × 0.85 = **0.373 → review**. With a
model agreeing at 0.8 the L1 would read backed(0.8, 0.438) ≈ 0.84.

---
## 4. Continuity: where one document ends and the next begins

Stage `continuity` (`core-pipeline/stages/lib/continuity/stage.py: run`), engine
`continuity/engine.py: assign`, settings `keyword-canon/continuity_canon.json`
(version 4). It writes only the grouping; Final values are written by `imaging_final`.

### 4.1 Overview

```
 pages in scan order:   p1     p2     p3     [blank]   p4     [junk]   p5
 gaps scored:              g12    g23           g34 (across)    g45 (across)
                           │
   each gap:  ending(p_before) + opening(p_after) + pair(p_before, p_after)  = score
              score > 0 break · < 0 joined · = 0 joined + flagged
              a decisive (5-point) signal settles it unless another opposes it
              rules: Demographics boundary → break; visit heading → break; Visual page → join
                           │
                           ▼
   segments (documents, junk groups) ──▶ final pass (split two label dates; merge weak
                                          breaks; Progress Note runs on) ──▶ tags, labels
```

**Gaps, not pages.** Every gap between two consecutive *scored* pages gets one break
score, a plain sum of points: `+` toward a break, `−` toward joined; 1 weak, 2
moderate, 3 strong, 5 decisive. A page's "Break After" and the next page's "Break
Before" are that same number.

**Scored pages** are Main pages and junk pages in a run of two or more. **Transparent**
pages — blank pages, a single junk page, duplicates — are not scored: the gap is scored
between the pages either side of them (a blank adds +1).

### 4.2 Inputs per page

`continuity/stage.run` builds one `engine.PageInput` per page. The key/value staging
(`staging/extraction.json`) is the first source for every fact; text patterns fill in.

| Field | Source |
|---|---|
| `kind` | blank_junk pass-2 flag: main / blank / junk / duplicate |
| `text` | best OCR text (blank and duplicate pages: none; pages settled in pass 1: Tesseract only) |
| `lines` with `top` / `bottom` | Final 2 line polygons (preferred), else Final 1 word polygons grouped into lines (`layout.lines_from_final1`: a word joins the line whose vertical middle it overlaps), else the text's lines with no position (`layout.page_layout`) |
| `section_headers` | the OCR JSON's matched section headers (with `top`) + the extraction's accepted `heading_heron` section rows |
| `page_type`, `names`, `page_confidence` | the **Extracted** L1 (`axes.family_of`), every name (`axes.names_of`: canon page type, L3, L2, L1) and `page_classification.confidence` |
| `title_hit` | the page has a `title_l1` (a canon title term heads it) |
| `dos_from`, `dos_to` | the page's Extracted DOS (Main pages only) |
| `dos_trust` | points for how that date was found: the best tier among the staged DOS rows that read it (`_staged_trust`): service / admit / discharge **5**; collected / received / weak (edge keyword) / printed **3**; keyless **2**; a date the staging does not explain **3** (`default`) |
| `signed`, `signed_in_tail` | any accepted e-signature row (one in the footer region signs the tail); else the provider-signature CSV; else the signature regex (electronically / digitally signed, signed by, authenticated by, provider / physician signature, "Signature:") on the page / its last third of lines |
| `printed` | "page N of M": the staged `page_no` row, else `additional_page_details.printed_page_no / total`; else read from the bands (4.3) |
| `visit_ids` | accepted `member_id` rows whose key is an encounter-type ID (FIN, CSN, account, visit) |
| `junk_subtype`, `junk_confidence` | for junk pages |
| `visual` | page tag `visual` with few words (`blank_junk.method.text_is_visual`) |

### 4.3 Bands and layout

`continuity_canon.json → bands`: with positions, a line whose top is above **0.10** of
the page height is header; a line whose bottom is below **0.92** is footer. Without
positions: the first **4** and last **3** lines; for pagination, a quarter of the lines
each way (`text_band_fraction` 0.25). Pagination patterns: `page N of|/ M`, `pg N of M`,
and the bare `N of M` (not inside a word, date or fraction), with 1 ≤ N ≤ M ≤ **20**
(`max_total`).

### 4.4 Every signal

`engine.score_gap`; points from `continuity_canon.json → points` (the engine refuses a
canon whose points name a signal it does not produce, or miss one). "a" is the earlier
page, "b" the later one.

| Signal | Side | Points | Condition / formula | Status (canon) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `different_dos` | pair | **+trust** (2, 3 or 5) | b is Main, both b and the open document have a DOS, and they differ; trust = min(trust(b), trust(open document's date)). **Decisive at 5** | assumed |
| `same_dos` | pair | **−trust** | the same, equal dates. Decisive at 5 | assumed |
| `pagination_follows` | pair | **−5, decisive** | printed(a) = (N, M) and printed(b) = (N+1, M) | measured (defines the ground truth) |
| `signed_in_tail` | ending | +3 | a signed in its closing lines | measured, difference 21 |
| `signed_anywhere` | ending | +2 | a signed anywhere | measured, difference 26 |
| `last_numbered_page` | ending | +3 | a prints "page N of N" | assumed |
| `says_continued` | ending | −3 | a's last 2 body lines end with continued / cont'd / see next page / (over) | assumed |
| `page_fill` | ending | 2 × fill(end) | where a's body text ends (bottom of its last line starting above the footer band), see the curve below; without positions: −2 when a has ≥ 28 lines or ≥ 1,800 characters (flagged when that decided the gap) | measured on the join side, difference 34 |
| `has_title` | opening | +3 | b has a canon title term heading it | assumed |
| `visit_heading` | opening | +3 (and a rule, 4.6) | a visit-opening heading (reason for visit, reason for appointment, visit information, visit info, chief complaint, cc, hpi, history of present illness) at the start of a line in b's top **33%** (first 8 lines without positions) or as a section heading there; a heading of 2+ words also at the **end** of a line (two-column OCR) | user rule 2026-10-23 |
| `text_start` | opening | 2 × start(gap) | space between b's header furniture and its first body line: ≤ 0.03 → −0.5, 0.03–0.12 → −0.5..0, 0.12–0.25 → 0..+1, ≥ 0.25 → +1. **Recorded only** (`apply: false`) | unmeasured |
| `page_type_change` | pair | 3 × (1 − same) × conf(b) | same = conf(b) when the L1s agree, else 1 − conf(b); added when ≥ 0.05. So two different L1s at conf(b) 0.9 → 3 × 0.81 = +2.43; the **same** L1 at conf(b) 0.81 → 3 × 0.19 × 0.81 = **+0.46** | assumed |
| `same_page_type` | pair | −1 | a and b have exactly the same name tuple (canon page type, L3, L2, L1) | deliberately weak |
| `sections_forward` | pair | −3 × forward | note-order ranks of a's and b's section headings (17 steps from chief complaint to follow up, `section_order`): forward = clip((min rank(b) − max rank(a)) / 17 × 4, 0, 1) | measured, difference 27, no false positives |
| `same_visit_number` | pair | −3 | a visit / encounter / FIN / CSN / account number (≥ 5 characters with a digit) on both | measured, difference 8 |
| `same_header_line` | pair | −2 | an identical header-band line (≥ 12 characters, digits → `#`, dates → `<date>`) | measured, difference 31 |
| `same_footer_line` | pair | −1 | the same in the footer band | measured, difference 8 |
| `wording_char` | pair | −2 × clip(cos / 0.25) | TF-IDF cosine of character 3–5-grams between a's last two body lines (extended back to 20 words) and b's first two (forward to 20 words), furniture lines removed, fitted on every tail / head block of the chart (`min_df` 2); added when ≥ 0.05 | measured, difference 27 |
| `wording_word` | pair | −1 × clip(cos / 0.15) | the same on word 1–2-grams | measured, difference 28, not independent |
| `cut_mid_sentence` | ending | −1 | only when `wording_char` fires: a's last body line has no terminal punctuation | tie-breaker only |
| `starts_mid_thought` | opening | −1 | only when `wording_char` fires: b's first body line, as read (`Line.raw`), starts with a lower-case letter. **[fixed 2026-10-28]**: it was tested on the lower-cased text, so it fired whenever `wording_char` did | tie-breaker only |
| `junk_run` | pair | +3 (+5 for a run of 3+) × the run's mean junk confidence | a gap at either end of a junk run; decisive for a run of 3+ when the scaled value ≥ 4.5 | assumed |
| `same_junk_kind` | pair | −2 | two junk pages of the same junk subtype | assumed |
| `blank_between` | pair | +1 | one or more blank pages in the gap | deliberately weak |

**The page-fill curve** (`signals.fill_value`; `page_fill` settings), where the text
ends as a fraction of the page height:

```
fill   +1 ┤              ┌──────────┐
          │             ╱            ╲
        0 ┼────────────╯              ╲─────────┐
          │                                      ╲
       −1 ┤                                       └──────
          └──┬────────┬──────┬───────┬────────┬────────
           0.35     0.50   0.65    0.80     0.92        end of text
           empty    band   band    taper    full
```

Below 0.35: too little to judge (0). 0.35–0.50 rises to +1; 0.50–0.65 the "ending
band" (+1); 0.65–0.80 falls to 0; 0.80–0.92 falls to −1; ≥ 0.92 a full page (−1, it
runs on). Times 2 points.

**The DOS comparison is against the open document.** `assign` keeps `open_dos`: the
first dated Main page of the current document. After a break into a Main page it is
reset; after a break into a junk group it stays open (a records-request cover between
two pages of one visit does not lose the visit's date).

### 4.5 Deciding a gap

`engine._decide`:

1. **total** = the sum of every signal (ending + opening + pair).
2. **Decisive signals** are `different_dos` / `same_dos` at trust 5,
   `pagination_follows`, and a decisive `junk_run`.
   * Decisive signals on both sides: a different date wins ("a date is a fact about the
     care; a page number about the printing"); else the join wins; `decided_by
     decisive_conflict`, level medium, flagged.
   * One side only: that side, `decisive`, level high.
3. **Else the sum:** break when total > 0; total = 0 joins and is flagged ("score 0:
   joined", `tie`). Level: |total| ≥ 5 high, ≥ 2 medium, else low.
4. **Conflict patterns**, recorded only (`conflict_rules.apply: false`): A = ending ≥ 4
   and pair ≤ −4 (a finished document followed by the same visit); B = opening ≥ 4 and
   pair ≤ −4 (a section heading taken for a title). Each drops the level one step and
   flags the gap.
5. **Confidence** (`_score_confidence`, `_ruled_confidence`; section 8):
   ```
   P(break)   = 1 / (1 + exp(−(total − b) / a))        a = 2, b = 0 (to be fitted)
   confidence = |2·P − 1|   when the score points the way the gap was decided, else 0
   × 0.8 (conflict) when the three sub-scores point opposite ways, two decisive signals
     disagree, or a pattern A / B fired
   decisive: max(that, 0.9); when the score points the other way by ≥ 2: 0.9 × 0.7
   ```
   Below 0.5, or level low: flagged "low confidence".

### 4.6 Rules that override the score

Applied after `_decide`, in this order (`score_gap`):

| Rule | When | Effect | Canon |
|---|---|---|---|
| Demographics boundary | a and b are Main and exactly one is a Demographics page (any of its names in `demographics_break.page_types`: Demographics, Patient Demographics, Patient Information, Patient Registration) | **break**, decisive, high; the final pass never merges it back | `demographics_break` |
| Visit heading | b has a visit heading (4.4), both Main, and `pagination_follows` did not fire | **break**, decisive, high | `visit_headings.decisive: true` |
| Visual page | b is a Visual page, both Main | **join**, decisive, high | `visual_join.enabled: true` |

Each sets the confidence by `_ruled_confidence` (at least 0.9 unless the score points
strongly the other way).

### 4.7 Building documents

`engine.assign` walks the scored pages in order: a break starts a new segment (Main or
junk), a join appends. A Main page joining a junk segment turns it into a Main
segment. Junk runs (`_junk_runs`): consecutive junk pages (blanks and duplicates in
between do not break a run) with the run's mean junk confidence. A single junk page is
transparent: when it sits between two pages of one document it is inside it; when it
sits at a break it **ends the document before it** (`_rows`). A blank page between two
documents belongs to neither. A duplicate joins its original's document.

### 4.8 The final pass

`engine._final_pass` (`final_pass` settings; every rule switchable in `rules`):

1. **Split: two label dates** (`split_two_dates`): inside a document, at each change
   between two different trust-5 dates, split (`final_pass: "split: two dates in one
   document"`, level medium).
2. **Merges across a weak break**, up to 3 passes. A break is weak when it is not
   decisive and its level is low or its score ≤ **2** (`weak_score`); never when the
   two documents carry different dates:
   * `junk_rejoin`: two junk segments of one junk kind;
   * `same_date`: the two documents' first dates are equal;
   * `continued_across`: the earlier document's last 2 body lines say "continued";
   * `same_type_untitled`: the same first-page name tuple and neither first page has a
     title;
   * `orphan_page`: a one-page undated document between two documents of the same type,
     both breaks weak → it joins the next.
3. **`progress_note_runs_on`** (`progress_note_families`: Progress Note): a document
   whose first Main page is a Progress Note **absorbs the next document**, again and
   again, unless the next is a junk group, the break is decisive, the next document's
   first page has a trust-5 date different from this document's, or this document's
   last page is signed. **It does not test whether the break is weak**: a break of +7.8
   is merged (4.10, variant). It is how a lab table or questionnaire after a note stays
   in the note; it is also how a consent after an unsigned note becomes part of it.

A gap a final-pass rule reverses gets confidence backed((1 − c) / 2, 0.9) × 0.7
(`_overturned`): always low.

### 4.9 Tags, links and labels

`engine._rows`, `_finish`:

* **tag**: `new` (first page of a segment), `continue`, `last`, `standalone` (one page).
* **label** (shown in review-ui): "New (Doc 3)", "Continue? (Doc 3)" (the ? when the
  gap before or after is level low), "Last (Junk 4)", "Blank (in Doc 3)". Documents and
  junk groups share one numbering (`document_seq`).
* **link_strength**: along a document from its first page, `strong` while every gap
  is joined by a −5 `pagination_follows` or `same_dos`, then `weak` for the rest. The
  Final step flags a page placed under another L1 across a weak link (5.4).
* **start_confirmed**: the first page has a title or prints page 1.
* **review_required**: any flag on the gap before or after; and every page of a Main
  document longer than **25** pages with no printed page number.
* **decided_by** per page: `chart_start`, `decisive`, `decisive_conflict`, `sum`, `tie`,
  `final_pass`, `attached` (a transparent page).
* **Carried values** (`document_finals`): the first page's Extracted L1 and encounter
  type, the first dated page's DOS; a stand-alone document whose every page is a
  `default_final_dos` type (Demographics, Problem List, Immunization ...) takes the
  non-visit date 2222-02-22.

### 4.10 Worked example: eight pages

Invented chart, scanned in this order. The facts per page are what `PageInput` would
receive; the scores are the real engine's output (`engine.assign`, canon version 4).

| Page | What it is | Extracted L1 (conf) | DOS (trust) | Facts |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Office visit, page 1 | Progress Note (0.88) | 2024-03-21 (5, "Date of Service") | title "Office Visit", "Page 1 of 2", Chief Complaint at 16% of the height, full page |
| 2 | Office visit, page 2 | Progress Note (0.81) | 2024-03-21 (5) | "Page 2 of 2", Assessment and Plan, Follow Up, **e-signed** at 56% |
| 3 | CMP result | Lab Report (0.82) | 2024-03-14 (3, "Collected") | lab letterhead, "Page 1 of 2" |
| 4 | CBC result | Lab Report (0.79) | 2024-03-14 (3) | same letterhead, "Page 2 of 2", text ends at 42% |
| 5 | Fax cover | junk (Fax Cover, 0.93) | — | single junk page |
| 6 | Release-of-information authorization | Consent and Authorization (0.74) | — | title |
| 7 | Office visit, next month | Progress Note (0.91) | 2024-04-18 (5) | title, "Reason for Visit:" at 12% |
| 8 | Its second page | Progress Note (0.66) | 2024-04-18 (5) | Assessment and Plan, e-signed |

**Gap by gap.** *Computed before the 2026-10-28 fix to `starts_mid_thought`.* With the
fix, none of these pages opens on a lower-case line, so the −1 on every opening goes, and
2→3 reads 0.997 rather than 0.80 (see below).

| Gap | Ending (a) | Opening (b) | Pair | Score | Decided | Conf. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1→2 | page_fill −2, cut_mid_sentence −1 | starts_mid_thought −1 | same_dos −5, **pagination_follows −5**, page_type_change +0.46, sections_forward −3, same_header_line −2, wording_char −2, wording_word −1 | **−21.54** | joined, decisive | 1.00 |
| 2→3 | signed_in_tail +3, signed_anywhere +2, last_numbered_page +3, page_fill +2, cut_mid_sentence −1 | starts_mid_thought −1 | different_dos **+3** (min trust 3: not decisive), page_type_change +2.02, wording_char −0.15, wording_word −0.06 | **+12.81** | break, sum, high | 0.80 |
| 3→4 | cut_mid_sentence −1 | starts_mid_thought −1 | same_dos −3, **pagination_follows −5**, page_type_change +0.50, same_header_line −2, wording_char −1.33, wording_word −0.77 | **−13.60** | joined, decisive | 1.00 |
| 4→6 (across 5) | last_numbered_page +3, page_fill +0.93, cut_mid_sentence −1 | has_title +3, starts_mid_thought −1 | page_type_change +1.64, wording_char −0.16 | **+6.41** | break, sum, high | 0.92 |
| 6→7 | page_fill +0.13, cut_mid_sentence −1 | has_title +3, **visit_heading +3**, starts_mid_thought −1 | page_type_change +2.48, wording_char −0.57, wording_word −0.13 | **+5.91** | break, **visit-heading rule** | 0.90 |
| 7→8 | cut_mid_sentence −1 | starts_mid_thought −1 | same_dos −5, page_type_change +0.67, sections_forward −3, same_header_line −2, wording_char −1.18, wording_word −0.63 | **−13.14** | joined, decisive | 1.00 |

How three of the numbers come about:

* **2→3, +12.81, confidence 0.80.** P(break) = 1 / (1 + e^−6.4) = 0.998, so the score
  alone is 0.997 sure. But the opening sub-score is −1 (the always-on
  `starts_mid_thought`) while ending (+9) and pair (+4.81) are positive: the sub-scores
  "disagree" and the 0.8 `conflict` penalty applies → 0.797. Without the defect (fixed
  2026-10-28) this gap reads 0.997.
* **2→3, different_dos +3.** The open document's date is 2024-03-21 (trust 5), the lab's
  2024-03-14 was read from "Collected" (trust 3): the lower trust counts, so the date
  change is strong but not decisive.
* **4→6, page_type_change +1.64.** L1s differ: same = 1 − 0.74 = 0.26, change = (1 −
  0.26) × 0.74 = 0.548, × 3 = 1.64. The fax cover (page 5) is transparent; being a
  single junk page at a break, it ends document 2.
* **1→2, page_type_change +0.46** although both pages are Progress Note: (1 − 0.81) ×
  0.81 × 3 (4.4).

**Documents.**

| Document | Pages | Tags |
|---|---|---|
| Doc 1 | 1, 2 | New, Last |
| Doc 2 | 3, 4, 5 (fax cover attached) | New, Continue, Last |
| Doc 3 | 6 | Standalone |
| Doc 4 | 7, 8 | New, Last |

The final pass changes nothing: no document holds two trust-5 dates, no break is weak,
and Doc 1's last page is signed, so `progress_note_runs_on` stops at once.

**Variant: page 2 is not signed.** Remove the signature line from page 2 and run again:

| Gap | Score | Before the final pass | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1→2 | −21.54 | joined | joined |
| 2→3 | **+7.78** (no signature points) | break, high | **joined: "merged: progress_note_runs_on"**, confidence 0.36 |
| 3→4 | −13.60 | joined | joined |
| 4→6 | **+6.41** | break, high | **joined: "merged: progress_note_runs_on"**, confidence 0.33 |
| 6→7 | +5.97 | break (visit heading, decisive) | break |
| 7→8 | −13.57 | joined | joined |

Doc 1 is now pages 1–6: the office visit, both lab pages, the fax cover and the
release-of-information form. Two breaks scored "high" were merged because the rule
does not ask whether a break is weak, and the consent's own title (+3) could not stop
it. Section 5.12 shows what this does to the Final page type; suggestion S1 proposes
the fix.

### 4.11 What is stored

| Where | Holds |
|---|---|
| `page_continuity_results` | per Main / junk page: `document_seq`, `seq`, tag, label, relation, `decided_by`, `break_before`, `break_after`, `score`, `confidence`, `confidence_level`, `link_strength`, `start_confirmed`, `page_no` / `page_total`, `review_required`, `evidence` (the strongest signals in words), `signals` (both gaps) |
| `staging/continuity.json` | `documents` (number, kind, carried page type and DOS, pages); per page `prev_to_this` / `this_to_next` in words ("same DOS −5", "visit heading 'reason for visit' +3") and `final`; then `encounters`, appended by the encounter stage (9.5) |
| `staging/continuity_detail.json` | per page every fact the scorer used (dos_trust, printed, signed, title, visit_ids) and both gaps in full: `ending`, `opening`, `pair`, `sub_scores`, `recorded` (text_start, conflict pattern), `flags`, `final_pass`, `evidence` |
| `imaging/<chart>_continuity.csv` | the same per page, for Local Mode, plus `layout_source` (final2 / final1 / text) |

### 4.12 Not built yet [to build]

The 2026-10-25 design, unchanged, still not in `engine.SIGNALS`:

* **`page_type_overlap` / `page_type_disjoint`** from the best paths instead of the top
  1: spread each page's stored path scores over L1s (P(L1) = Σ scores of its paths with
  that L1 / Σ all), overlap = Σ min(P_a, P_b); ≥ 0.60 → −2, ≤ 0.10 with both top paths
  ≥ 0.60 → +2; the embedded pairs (Progress Note ↔ Lab Report / Medication Record /
  Order and Requisition, Operative ↔ Procedure Note, Discharge Summary ↔ Lab Report)
  0. Would replace `page_type_change` and `same_page_type`. Note 3.7: `paths` scores
  favour shallow paths; normalise per level before using them (S8).
* **`encounter_change`**, needing the encounter's Extracted vote before continuity:
  Inpatient ↔ Outpatient with strong evidence +3; one outpatient modality ↔ another +2;
  the same setting stated on both −1; weaker evidence 0.
* **Layout fingerprint** from the OCR boxes (header / footer zones with masked digits
  and horizontal anchors, left margin, right edge, body line height, columns, page
  size): shared zones −2 (`same_layout`), a changed template +2 (`layout_change`), a
  changed page size +1. Not for handwritten, visual, form pages or pages under 8 lines.
* **Measuring the weights** (S3): every "assumed" weight above.

---
## 5. Final page type, subtype and specialty

Stage `imaging_final` (`core-pipeline/stages/lib/imaging_final/stage.py: run`), the
last stage. The page type is decided by `page_classify/arbitration.py: decide_final`
with its tables in `page_arbitration.json`. Extracted is never overwritten: a reviewer
and the accuracy report see both.

```
 continuity documents (blank / junk / duplicate pages left out)
        │  packets(): a short packet ending in a Consent page absorbs the short documents before it
        ▼
 per document: agree_document_type()  →  the document's L1 (last_page / first_page / vote / no_agreement)
        │
        ▼
 per page: decide_final()
    its own L1 = the agreed L1 → keeps its own L2 / L3                          (agreed)
    another L1 / Others       → _names_under(agreed L1): final_placement,
                                 Progress Note → Others, under_l1, own name, Others   (agreed_<basis>)
    alone (no / one-page doc) → keeps its own; embedded_same_page exception      (no_change / embedded_same_page)
        │
        ▼
 codability from the Final path's taxonomy tag · specialty from the document's first page
 DOS from its first dated page · encounter type from its encounter · confidences · needs_review
```

### 5.1 What Final starts from

Each page's Extracted answer, read from `page_classification` (`_final_page`):
`page_type` / `page_subtype` as written, `family` (its L1), `names` (canon page type,
L3, L2, L1), its own `l2`, `under_l1` (its best L2 / L3 under every other L1),
`title_l1`, `confidence`; and from `page_continuity_results` its `document_seq`, `seq`
and `link_strength`. The page rules (3.5) have already run on Extracted.

### 5.2 The documents used

`imaging_final._documents`: continuity's documents, with blank, junk and duplicate
pages left out (they share the numbering but keep their own values). For the **page
type only**, `arbitration.packets` then joins short packets
(`page_arbitration.json → last_page_documents`): a document whose **last page** is
Consent and Authorization absorbs the documents right before it while the whole
packet stays within **4 pages** and every absorbed page is Consent and Authorization,
Administrative, Demographics or Forms and Questionnaires (continuity may have split a
notice from its signed page, and it always splits a Demographics page off). DOS,
encounter type and specialty keep continuity's documents.

### 5.3 The document's L1

`arbitration.agree_document_type`, the first that applies:

| Basis | When | Agreed L1 | `continuation_rule` on re-placed pages | Review |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **last_page** | the (packet) document has ≤ 4 pages and its last page is Consent and Authorization | Consent and Authorization | `agreed_last_page` | weak link only |
| **first_page** | the first page's L1 is known (not Others, not Unknown) | the first page's | `agreed_first_page` | weak link only |
| **vote** | the first page is Others / Unknown: the pages' L1s weighted by their page-type confidence (0.5 when none), Others / Unknown left out; one L1 holds **≥ 60%** of the weight (`document_agreement.vote_min_share`) | the winner; a tie goes to the first page's L1, else the earliest | `agreed_vote` | **always** |
| **no_agreement** | no L1 reaches 60% | — every page keeps its own | `no_agreement` | always |

A document of one page is not agreed (5.5).

### 5.4 Placing each page

A page whose own L1 **is** the agreed L1 keeps its page type and subtype (`agreed`;
source `page`). A page of another L1, or Others, is re-placed under the agreed L1 by
`arbitration._names_under`, **the first that applies**:

1. **`final_placement[agreed][own L1]`**, the embedded-page table:

   | Document L1 | Page's own L1 | Final L2 |
   |---|---|---|
   | Progress Note | Lab Report | Lab and Imaging Results |
   | Progress Note | Medication Record | Medication List |
   | Progress Note | Problem List | Problem List |
   | Progress Note | Forms and Questionnaires | Questionnaire |
   | Operative Note | Procedure Note | Intra-operative Record |

2. Inside a **Progress Note** (the section L1), any other page: **Progress Note /
   Others** (a note's L2 is a content section, which a foreign page does not have).
3. Under any other L1: the L2 (and L3) the classifier gave the page under that L1
   (`under_l1`; written "L1 (L2)" / L3 or L1 / L2).
4. Its own L2 or one of its names, when the taxonomy places it under that L1.
5. Otherwise **L1 / Others**. Never "L1 / L1".

These pages are source `continuation`; they need review when the basis is `vote`, when
their continuity link is `weak`, or when the Final path's codability is unsure.

### 5.5 A page alone

A page in no document, or in a one-page document, keeps its own answer (`no_change`),
with one exception, **`embedded_same_page`**: a page of an embedded family (Lab Report,
Medication Record, Immunization, Problem List, Order and Requisition;
`inside_progress_note.embedded_families`) whose **title** names Progress Note
(`title_l1`) becomes Progress Note with its step-1 / step-2 L2 (Lab Report → Lab and
Imaging Results; Immunization or Order and Requisition → Others). Source `embedded`.

### 5.6 Subtype: codability

**Codability comes from the Final path, never from Extracted** (2026-10-25, user):
`arbitration._final_codability` → `final_tag` → `taxonomy.codeability_of`, first that
applies:

1. no Final L1 (Others / Unknown): the page's own Extracted category;
2. the family is fixed in code (`taxonomy.L1_CODEABILITY`): Progress Note and Operative
   Note **Codable**; Consent and Authorization, Administrative, Demographics
   **Non-Codable**;
3. the taxonomy tag (`page_taxonomy.json → codeability`) of `L1 > L2 > L3`, else of
   `L1 > L2`, else of `L1 > Others`, else the one tag every L2 of the L1 shares;
4. a path tagged `needs_review` (none today) would take the document's codability (the
   Final tag of the page that set the L1), else stay Non-Codable flagged.

The tags today (166 tagged paths; `docs/codeability_review.xlsx` lists them):

| Family | Final codability |
|---|---|
| Progress Note (every L2, Lab and Imaging Results and Medication List included) | **Codable** (fixed in code) |
| Operative Note | **Codable** (fixed in code) |
| Procedure Note (15 paths) | **Codable** (tags) |
| Lab Report > Radiology Report > CT Angiography Report, MR Angiography Report | **Codable** (tags) |
| Lab Report > Pathology Report (the L2 alone) | **Codable** (tag) — while every L3 under it (Biopsy, Cytology, Cytometry, Molecular Diagnostics, Others) is Non-Codable **[decide]** |
| Discharge Summary (10 paths) | **Discharge** |
| Consent and Authorization, Administrative, Demographics | **Non-Codable** (fixed in code) |
| Every other path (the rest of Lab Report, Clinical Summary, Immunization, Medication Record, Order and Requisition, Problem List, Forms and Questionnaires, Provider Letter, Patient Communication) | **Non-Codable** |

The Pathology inconsistency matters only when a Final path stops at that L2: the
Extracted gate always adds an L3 (Others at worst), but `_names_under` writes "Lab
Report / Pathology Report" when `under_l1["Lab Report"]` carries no L3.

`imaging_final.codability` is the label (Codable / Non-Codable / Discharge) and
`classification_category` its code (codeable / non_codeable / discharge_summary).

### 5.7 Specialty

**Extracted** (3.9): the model behind its gate, else the specialty keywords, else the
canon entry, else General.

**Final** (`imaging_final.encounter_specialties`): **only the first page of the
continuity document decides**, and every page of the document takes it, so the
specialty never changes midway (2026-10-25, user). A first page with none, Unknown or
Others gives **General**. A page outside a document keeps its own (General when it has
none). Continuity's documents are used, not the page-type packets.

Consequence worth knowing: a cardiology note whose first page is a generic intake page
(specialty General) reads General on every page, even when page 2's letterhead says
"Heart Institute". `specialty_canon.json` carries `inherit_across_document: true`
("a specialty found on any page of a document applies to every page"), but no code
reads that key (S12).

### 5.8 Blank, junk and duplicate pages

* **Blank / junk**: no page type; the junk label (Blank, Invoice, Fax Cover ...) is the
  subtype; Non-Codable; their own DOS / encounter values.
* **Duplicate**: copies its original's Final content (`COPIED_FROM_REFERENCE`): member,
  `document_seq`, page type, subtype, specialty, model type, codability, category,
  continuation rule, needs_review, DOS, encounter type, provider and signature values;
  keeps its own image values, its blank / junk flag and its `seq`.
  `page_type_source` = `dos_source` = `duplicate`, `duplicate_of_page_id` names the
  original. Content confidences are the original's.

### 5.9 DOS and encounter carried

`continuity.engine.document_finals` on each continuity document (Main pages only):
**DOS** = the first dated page's (`dos_source` document); a stand-alone document whose
every page is a `default_final_dos` type takes the non-visit date **2222-02-22**.
**Encounter type** = the document's first page's stored encounter type, which is the
encounter's decision (section 6). A page outside a document keeps its own.

### 5.10 Confidence and review

`imaging_final.final_confidences`, stored in `imaging_final.confidences`:

| Value | Confidence |
|---|---|
| page type | the deciding page's `page_classification.confidence` (the page itself when it kept its type; else the document's first page), × 0.8 (`conflict`) when another page of the document is ≥ 0.80 sure of another L1 |
| codability | as sure as the deciding page's L1 (its `codeability_confidence`, with the page's handwritten / thin-text doubt), × conflict as above |
| specialty | the document's first page's `specialty_confidence` |
| DOS | the document's dated page's; none for the non-visit default date |
| encounter type | the encounter's (its first page's) |
| member, provider, blank_junk, handwriting | the page's own |

`review_fields` lists the values under 0.50; `needs_review` is set when any is, when
`decide_final` said so (vote, no agreement, weak link, unsure codability), or when the
Extracted page type was nobody's (decided Others or no text).

### 5.11 What is written

Per page in `imaging_final` and `imaging/<chart>_final.csv`: `page_type`,
`page_subtype`, `model_type`, `codability`, `classification_category`, `specialty`,
`page_type_source` (page / continuation / embedded / duplicate), `continuation_rule`
(no_change / agreed / agreed_first_page / agreed_vote / agreed_last_page /
embedded_same_page / no_agreement), `needs_review`, `document_seq`, `dos_from` /
`dos_to` / `dos_source`, `encounter_type`, `seq`, `duplicate_of_page_id`, the member,
provider and image values, `confidences`, `review_fields`.

### 5.12 Worked examples: Extracted ≠ Final

**1. The eight-page chart of 4.10, page 2 unsigned** (`decide_final` run on the merged
document 1 = pages 1, 2, 3, 4, 6; the fax cover is junk and keeps its own values):
