| Page | Extracted | Final | Codability | Source / rule | Review |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Progress Note / Others | Progress Note / Others | Codable | page / agreed | no |
| 2 | Progress Note / Assessment and Plan | Progress Note / Assessment and Plan | Codable | page / agreed | no |
| 3 | Lab Report (Lab Result) / Chemistry Report | **Progress Note / Lab and Imaging Results** | **Codable** | continuation / agreed_first_page | yes (weak link) |
| 4 | Lab Report (Lab Result) / Hematology Report | **Progress Note / Lab and Imaging Results** | **Codable** | continuation / agreed_first_page | yes |
| 6 | Consent and Authorization / Release of Information | **Progress Note / Others** | **Codable** | continuation / agreed_first_page | yes |
| 7, 8 | Progress Note / CC/HPI, Assessment and Plan | unchanged (document 2) | Codable | page / agreed | no |

Why: the first page is a Progress Note, so the document agrees on Progress Note
(`first_page`). The lab pages are placed by `final_placement` (Lab Report → Lab and
Imaging Results); the consent has no placement inside a Progress Note → Others. All
three read Codable because every Progress Note path is Codable. Pages 3, 4 and 6 are
flagged: their links are weak (no −5 join behind them). In the signed version of the
chart (4.10, main run) none of this happens: the labs are their own Lab Report document
(Non-Codable) and the consent stands alone (Non-Codable). **The Final codability of
three pages depends on one signature line**: the reason for S1.

**2. First page Others: the vote.** A four-page document: page 1 Others (0.30), page 2
Lab Report (0.80), page 3 Lab Report (0.70), page 4 Progress Note (0.60). Others is left
out; weights Lab Report 1.50, Progress Note 0.60 → Lab Report holds 71% ≥ 60% →
**Lab Report**, `agreed_vote`, every re-placed page flagged. Page 1 takes its
`under_l1["Lab Report"]`, e.g. "Lab Report (Lab Result)" / Hematology Report; page 4
(Progress Note) has no `final_placement` row under Lab Report, so it also takes its
`under_l1["Lab Report"]` L2 (say Lab Result) and becomes Non-Codable. Had page 3 been
0.40 and page 4 1.00: Lab Report 1.20 vs Progress Note 1.00 = 55% < 60% →
`no_agreement`, every page keeps its own type, all flagged.

**3. A consent packet decided by its last page.** Continuity gives document A = one
Demographics page (the Demographics rule always splits it off) and document B = a
privacy notice page (Extracted Forms and Questionnaires) + the signed consent (Consent
and Authorization). `packets`: B's last page is Consent; A has 1 page, all
Demographics (a `joins` family); 1 + 2 ≤ 4 → one packet of 3. `agree_document_type`:
last page Consent, 3 ≤ 4 → **Consent and Authorization** (`agreed_last_page`). The
Demographics page becomes Consent and Authorization / its `under_l1` L2 (e.g. Consent
for Treatment); the notice likewise. All Non-Codable. Their DOS and encounter still
come from continuity's two documents.

**4. A one-page lab result headed "Office Visit".** A page alone (its own document)
Extracted Lab Report (Lab Result) / Glucose Report, whose title zone starts with
"Office Visit" (`title_l1` Progress Note): `embedded_same_page` → **Progress Note / Lab
and Imaging Results**, Codable, source `embedded`.

**5. A procedure page inside an operative document.** Document opened by an Operative
Note page; page 2 Extracted Procedure Note / Office Procedure → `final_placement`
**Operative Note / Intra-operative Record**, Codable either way.

**6. A Progress Note page inside a Discharge Summary.** Document opened by Discharge
Summary / Depart/Discharge Note; page 3 Extracted Progress Note / Others. No
`final_placement` row under Discharge Summary → its `under_l1["Discharge Summary"]`,
e.g. Consultation Note → **Discharge Summary / Consultation Note**, codability
**Discharge** (was Codable). Specialty of every page: the first page's.

**7. A duplicate.** Page 12 is "Duplicate (To Page 4)": Extracted takes page 4's page
type (and stays Non-Codable); Final copies page 4's Final page type, subtype,
codability, specialty, DOS and encounter; `page_type_source` duplicate.

### 5.13 Context rules: built and not built

The taxonomy's context rules (appendix C) describe the document view:

| Rule | Status |
|---|---|
| procedure_in_operative_document | **built**: `final_placement` (5.4) |
| absorb_into_progress_note (lab, medication, problem list, forms inside a note) | **built**: `final_placement`; plus continuity's `progress_note_runs_on` (4.8) |
| page_essence | **built**: the labels and Extracted (section 2) |
| clinical_summary | **built** on the page (title rule, 3.5), not from the document |
| admission_history_and_physical / inpatient_consultation | **built** on the page (stay evidence on the page itself); evidence elsewhere in the admission is not read |
| notice_inside_consent_packet | **built**: `last_page_documents` (5.2, 5.3) |
| specialty_from_page_type | **built**: the canon entry's specialty (3.9, rule 3) |
| telephone_encounter, superbill, records_request | canon page types and strong terms on the page; **no document rule** |
| discharge_diagnosis_page, combined_endoscopy, dme_documents, interventional_radiology_procedure, scheduling_form | **not built [decide]** |

---
## 6. Encounter type

Stage `encounter_type` (`encounter/stage.py: run`), logic
`encounter/encounter_classify.py: classify_pages`, canon `encounter_canon.json`, model
`models/encounter/` (DistilBERT, trained by `training/bert-training-encounter`).

Values (`settings`, with their tie priority): Home (10), Outpatient (Tele) (20),
Outpatient (F2F) (30), Inpatient (40), plus Not Available (`not_applicable`). The
model's classes are named Outpatient (Evisit) / Outpatient (Home Visit); the stored
label for tele is "Outpatient (Tele)".

### 6.1 The encounter is the continuity document

`group_visits`: when continuity has run, one encounter per continuity document
(consecutive pages with the same `document_seq`); otherwise runs of pages sharing a
date. Blank / junk / duplicate pages are skipped. The encounter's **family** is its
first page's **Extracted** L1 (`encounter_family`; a page decided Others has none, so
the next page with one counts).

### 6.2 Each page's vote

`page_vote`, the first that applies:

```
 family not in applies_to (Progress Note, Discharge Summary, Operative Note)? ──▶ Not Available   (not_applicable)
 evidence on the page (gather + decide):
     tier 1: the page's most specific name listed in tier1_page_types (Discharge Summary,
             ICU Note, Operative Note / Report, Surgery Report, Critical Care Record,
             Ventilator management record → Inpatient; Office Visit, Urgent care note →
             F2F; Telephone Encounter → Tele; HouseCalls visit summary → Home)
     tier 2: a phrase naming the setting ("telehealth", "video visit", "admission date",
             "hospital course", "home visit", "office visit", ...)
     negatives cancel named findings or a whole setting (tier 1 too when "tier1": true:
             ambulatory surgery center, discharged home same day → no Inpatient)
     two settings in the deciding tier: the one with more tier 3 hints, then priority
   ── a tier 2 F2F phrase gives way to the model at ≥ 0.95 for Tele / Home / Inpatient
   ──▶ that setting (tier1 / tier2)
 the model: Tele / Home / Inpatient at p ≥ 0.50, or ≥ 0.30 with one of the setting's
            confirm words on the page (video, telephone, mychart, POS 02 / 10 / 12,
            homebound, assisted living, admitted, ICU, telemetry, ...)  ──▶ (model)
 family default: Progress Note → F2F, Discharge Summary → Inpatient ──▶ (family_default)
 else no vote
```

### 6.3 The encounter's decision

`classify_pages`, the first that applies:

1. **The family decides** (`family_decision`): Discharge Summary and Operative Note are
   **always Inpatient** (`applies_to.always`); a family not in `applies_to` (Lab Report,
   Order, Form ...) is **Not Available** for the whole encounter. `decided_by` family /
   not_applicable.
2. **The first page decides** when its vote has evidence of its own (tier 1, tier 2 or
   the model): `decided_by first_page`; `conflict` when another page with evidence says
   otherwise.
3. **The vote** (`tally`): the first page weighs **2**; only pages with evidence vote
   when any has it, else the family defaults vote; Not Available is set aside when any
   setting is voted; the weighted majority wins, a tie goes to the first page's vote,
   then priority.
4. Nothing voted: the canon default **Outpatient (F2F)** (`decided_by default`), a
   fallback.

Every page of the encounter is stamped with the decision (`encounter_type_results`,
with `encounter_seq`, `decided_by`, `reason`, `conflict`, `contenders`).

### 6.4 Worked example (real `classify_pages`; model answers assumed)

| Encounter | Pages | Evidence | Decision | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | p1 Progress Note: "Telehealth visit conducted via secure video"; p2: "Office visit follow up in 2 weeks" | p1 tier 2 Tele ("telehealth"); p2 tier 2 F2F ("office visit") | **Outpatient (Tele)**, first_page, conflict | model p(Tele) 0.35 → backed(0.35, 0.8) = 0.61; the model's own answer is F2F 0.55 ≥ 0.5 → × 0.7 = 0.427; conflict × 0.8 = **0.34** |
| 2 | p3 Operative Note: "Example Ambulatory Surgery Center ... discharged home same day" | the negatives cancel the tier 1 and tier 2 Inpatient findings; the page itself votes **F2F** | **Inpatient**, family ("Operative Note: always Inpatient") | backed(p(Inpatient) 0.25, 1.0) = 0.625 × 0.7 (model said F2F) = **0.44** |
| 3 | p4 Lab Report | — | **Not Available**, not_applicable | 0.975 |
| 4 | p5, p6 Progress Note, no setting words | family defaults (F2F) | **Outpatient (F2F)**, vote | p5 0.72 × 0.6 = 0.432 (weight 2), p6 0.60 × 0.6 = 0.36 → mean 0.408 × share 1.0 = **0.41** |

Encounter 2 shows the defect of "What changed" row 11: the ASC negative works on the
page, but the family rule runs first and the encounter stays Inpatient (S6).

### 6.5 Where it is stored, and how good it is

`encounter_type_results` per page; `staging/continuity.json → encounters` per encounter
(seq, document, page family, pages, dates, setting, confidence, decided_by, reason,
matched keyword, conflict, contenders — not the per-page votes); the votes, findings,
cancelled findings and negatives only in `debug/<chart>_encounter_evidence.csv` with
`ENCOUNTER_DEBUG`. Measured numbers: section 11. **[to build]**: the page's own vote
before continuity (4.12); the client default (an outpatient encounter with no stated,
field or exam / vitals evidence of the modality → Outpatient (Evisit)); splitting a
document whose pages state two settings and carry two label dates.

---

## 7. Page sequencing

Stage `page_sequencing` (`core-pipeline/stages/lib/sequencing/`), after encounter type,
before `imaging_final`. It suggests a reading order and never moves or renames a file:
**current sequence** is scan order (`page_number`), **actual sequence** the suggestion
(`page_sequencing_results.seq`, copied to `imaging_final.seq`).

* **Two series.** Main pages are numbered 1 … n with no gaps. Blank, junk and duplicate
  pages are numbered **J1, J2 …** in scan order (`seq` NULL, method `blank_junk`) and
  never enter the ordering.
* **Markers.** One printed page number per page: the key/value extraction's
  `printed_page_no` / `total` first (confidence 1.0 with a total, 0.85 without), else
  footer, header, body patterns ("Page 3 of 7", "Pg 3", "p. 3 of 7", "3 of 7", "3/7",
  "- 3 -", a lone footer number; 1.0 for patterns that say "page", 0.92 bare, 0.85 no
  total, 0.78 lone footer number). Markers are grouped into families by their total; a
  bare number on one page alone ("1/25") is a date or a dose and is dropped.
* **Order: scan order, except confident "page n of m".** Pages that confidently say
  page n of m with the same m are one family and take the family's scan positions in
  page-number order, moving at most **5 places** (`detect.WINDOW`), unless the whole
  document is numbered (then it is put in order however far: a document scanned in
  reverse is reversed back). Two pages claiming one number, or a move too far: the
  family stays in scan order, flagged (`marker_held`). Methods: `explicit_marker`,
  `explicit_marker_reference` (total ≠ Main page count, capped 0.90), `marker_held`,
  `header_group`, `original_order`, `blank_junk`.
* **Pages with no marker** keep scan order; header groups (Jaccard of header / footer
  words > 0.70) are recorded (0.90 with a marker in the group, else 0.75). The
  cross-encoder continuation model is **off** (`SEQUENCING_CROSS_ENCODER` false) and its
  ONNX file is not in `sequencing/artifacts/` (only the tokenizer is).
* **Accuracy** (review-ui Sequencing tab): Main pages on both sides only; a page is a
  break when it is not the ground truth's next page after the page before it; accuracy
  = 1 − breaks / Main pages compared.
* **Open:** continuity runs before sequencing and reads scan order, so a chart scanned
  out of order splits documents where pages are out of place [decide].

---

## 8. Confidence scores

Every decided value carries a confidence in [0, 1] on one scale
(`keyword-canon/confidence_canon.json`, applied by `stages/lib/confidence.py`):
**≥ 0.80 good · 0.50–0.80 passable · < 0.50 review** (flagged; review-ui colours the
cell red and says Review).

```
base       = the model's probability for the decided value
             (no model: the rule's own strength × no_model)
backed     = base + (1 − base) × 0.5 × support          support ∈ [0, 1] from agreeing keywords / rules
score      = backed × Π penalties                        then per-module calibration (none fitted yet), clip [0, 1]
```

| Penalty | × | When |
|---|---|---|
| `keywords_disagree` | 0.70 | keywords / rules point to another value (or a rule overrides the model's favourite) |
| `no_model` | 0.85 | decided without a model |
| `fallback` | 0.60 | a default filled it (Others, General, a family default, scan order nothing confirms) |
| `conflict` | 0.80 | other pages / sources of the same document say something else |
| `overturned` | 0.70 | a later pass reversed the first decision |
| `handwritten` | 0.90 | the page is handwritten or mixed |
| `thin_text` | 0.85 | under 150 characters of text |
| `degraded` | 0.60 | an optional dependency was missing |

Per module, as used in sections 3–6:

| Value | Base | Lifted by | Penalised for |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1 / L2 / L3 (Extracted) | the model's p (L2, L3 given the parent) | the top keyword class's share × strength; a pin or a rule fully | keywords elsewhere with strength ≥ 0.5; a rule against the model; Others: p(OTHER) or (1 − best) × fallback |
| Page type | product of the named levels except the deepest | — | no_model once; handwritten; thin text |
| Specialty | the model's p | the specialty keywords (score / 12) | keywords naming another; General: (1 − best named) × fallback |
| Codeability | the L1's | — | — |
| Continuity gap | \|2σ(score / 2) − 1\| | a decisive signal or rule holds it at ≥ 0.90 | sub-scores or decisive signals disagreeing × conflict; a rule against the score 0.9 × 0.7; a final-pass reversal: backed((1 − c)/2, 0.9) × overturned |
| Encounter page vote | the model's p for the setting, normalised over the settings | tier 1 support 1.0, tier 2 0.8 (0.6 in conflict), a confirm word 0.5 | the model's own answer (≥ 0.5) another setting × 0.7; a family default × fallback; no model: support × no_model |
| Encounter | the first page's vote, or share of the weight × the voters' mean confidence | — | another page with evidence disagreeing × conflict |
| Final page type, codability | the deciding page's | — | another page of the document ≥ 0.80 sure of another L1 × conflict |
| Final specialty, encounter | the document's first page's | — | — |

No module is calibrated yet (`calibration: {}`): the numbers are honest probabilities
and penalties, not measured hit rates (S10).

---
## 9. How the JSON files help

Three kinds of JSON shape these decisions:

* the **editable canon** (`core-pipeline/stages/lib/keyword-canon/*.json`): every word
  list, weight, threshold and table the stages use. Change behaviour without a code
  change;
* the **per-chart staging** (`<chart>/staging/*.json`): what each stage decided and
  why, page by page; read by later stages, review-ui and people debugging;
* the **training JSON** (`training_data.jsonl`, `encounter_tags.jsonl`,
  `label_space.json`, `metrics_summary.json`): how labelled pages become a model.

### 9.1 Map of the files

| File | Read by | Holds | Editing it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `page_taxonomy.json` | `page_classify/taxonomy.py` | the v29.2 `label_space` (L1, L2 per L1, L3 per "L1 > L2", specialties), `codeability` per path (166), the conversion tables, `context_rules` | the class lists the vote can name; Extracted and **Final codability** of a path |
| `page_canon.json` | `axes` (`canon_entry`, `title_l1`), `keyword_vote.fit` | 348 canon page types: path, codeability tag and rule, specialty, lexicon (strong / title / body / ambiguous terms); `weights`, `title_zone_lines` (12) | at once: the canon page type behind a path, `title_l1` (continuity's `has_title`, `embedded_same_page`), specialty from the canon. **The vote only after `fit`** |
| `class_keywords.json` | `page_classify/class_keywords.py` (merges into `page_canon.json`) | curated strong / body words per L1, L2, L3 path (1,236 words over 163 classes) | nothing until merged and refitted |
| `page_keyword_weights.json` | `keyword_vote` | the vote's `settings` (share, full, min per level); `strong` (pins); `L1` / `L2` / `L3` feature → class → weight | at once: the vote, pins, Others threshold |
| `page_arbitration.json` | `axes`, `page_rules`, `arbitration` | `axes` (taxonomy version, specialty gate 0.50 / 0.10 / 10 pages, fallback General), `axes_rules` (clinical_summary, hospital_stay, lab_guard), `final_placement`, `document_agreement` (0.60), `last_page_documents`, `inside_progress_note`, `confidence` (canon's own) | Extracted rules and specialty gate; the **Final** page type |
| `specialty_canon.json` | `specialty_keywords` | `matching` (top band 0.18, header 6, body 1, min 6, margin 3), header / body terms per specialty | Extracted specialty from words |
| `continuity_canon.json` | `continuity.engine` | pagination patterns, bands, `points` of every signal, `dos_trust`, page-fill and text-start curves, semantic settings, `section_order`, `visit_headings`, `demographics_break`, `default_final_dos`, `decision`, `conflict_rules`, `final_pass` | where documents begin and end; the non-visit DOS types |
| `encounter_canon.json` | `encounter_classify` | settings and priorities, `tier1_page_types`, tier 2 / tier 3 phrases, negatives, `applies_to` (+ `always`), `vote` (first page weight 2, family defaults), `model_vote` (classes, 0.50, confirm words, 0.30, override 0.95), `default` | the encounter type |
| `confidence_canon.json` | `stages/lib/confidence.py` | bands (0.80 / 0.50), `agree_lift` 0.5, the penalties, `calibration` | every confidence and review flag |
| `section_header_canon.json` | `ocr/section_header_match` (stage 6) | section header catalogue | the OCR JSON's `section_headers` → continuity's `sections_forward`, `visit_heading`, and the page classifier's heading lines (after `only: ["section_headers"]`) |
| `dos_canon.json` | DOS stage; `continuity` (non-visit date) | DOS labels and weights; `DOS_NON_VISIT_DATE` 2222-02-22, `DOS_DEFAULT_DATE` | each page's DOS and its tier → continuity's date signals |

### 9.2 Reload on change

Every canon file is read through `stages/lib/canon_store.py: CanonFile`:

```
CanonFile(path, build).get():
   at most once a second (CHECK_INTERVAL_SEC = 1.0): stat the file
   mtime unchanged → the cached value
   mtime changed   → read JSON, run build (compile regexes, check the points table ...)
       ok      → swap in the new value, log "<file> changed — reloaded"
       error   → log it, KEEP the last good version (and do not retry that same file)
   first load fails → raise (no good version to fall back on)
```

So an edit takes effect **on the next page processed**: no restart, no re-OCR. A typo
cannot take down a running batch; check the log line ("changed — reloaded" or "could
not be loaded — keeping last good version"). Some builds validate: `continuity_canon.json`
refuses a `points` table that names a signal the engine does not produce, or misses
one; `encounter_canon.json` checks that every tier 1 page type is a real page name.

Two limits:

* **Docker**: the canon folder is part of the image (the compose file mounts only the
  chart workspace, metadata, models and logs). On the VM, edit the file inside the
  running container or rebuild the image; a host edit alone changes nothing there.
* **Already-processed pages do not change.** A reload affects the next page a stage
  processes. To apply an edit to a chart already run, re-run the stages that read the
  file (9.4).

### 9.3 Example edits and their effect

**1. Let the Progress Note subtype be named more often.** In example C no section
reached the L2 minimum (best 0.160). In `page_keyword_weights.json`:

```json
"settings": {
  "full":  {"L1": 6.0, "L2": 1.0, "L3": 6.0},
  "min":   {"L1": 0.2, "L2": 0.15, "L3": 0.2},
  "share": {"L1": 0.2, "L2": 0.5, "L3": 0.2}
}
```

Effect: the page reads Progress Note / **Physical Exam** (0.160 ≥ 0.15), with a
subtype confidence of 0.160 (it does not flag the page). Every L2 in every family
moves too; measure on the validation pages first (`keyword_vote evaluate`).

**2. Add a strong term — and refit.** To make a page titled "Encounter Form" pin
Administrative > Superbill, add it to the Superbill entry of `page_canon.json`:

```json
{"page_type": "Superbill",
 "path": {"L1": "Administrative", "L2": "Superbill", "L3": ""},
 "codeability": "non_codeable", "codeability_rule": "page_type_tag",
 "lexicon": {"strong_terms": ["Superbill", "Super Bill", "Encounter Form"], ...}}
```

Effect **before refit**: none on the vote (no weight, no pin); `canon_entry` and
`title_l1` see it at once. After `python -m stages.lib.page_classify.keyword_vote fit
--data training_data.jsonl --split <run>/split.csv` the weights file gains
`"encounter form|title": ["Administrative"]` under `strong.L1` (and Superbill under
`strong.L2.Administrative`), and the next page titled "Encounter Form" is pinned. A
refit also re-learns every weight from the pages: review the diff.

**3. Stop a note from swallowing what follows it.** In `continuity_canon.json`:

```json
"final_pass": {"rules": {"progress_note_runs_on": false, ...}}
```

Effect on the 4.10 variant: the +7.78 and +6.41 breaks stand; documents become
{1, 2}, {3, 4, 5}, {6}, {7, 8}; Final: the labs stay Lab Report (Non-Codable) and the
consent stays Consent and Authorization. Every Progress Note document that relied on
the rule to keep a trailing lab or questionnaire page loses it too: S1 proposes a
narrower rule instead.

**4. Place a Progress Note page inside a Discharge Summary.** In
`page_arbitration.json`:

```json
"final_placement": {
  "Progress Note": {"Lab Report": "Lab and Imaging Results", "Medication Record": "Medication List",
                    "Problem List": "Problem List", "Forms and Questionnaires": "Questionnaire"},
  "Operative Note": {"Procedure Note": "Intra-operative Record"},
  "Discharge Summary": {"Progress Note": "Interim Summary"}
}
```

Effect: in 5.12 example 6 the page reads Discharge Summary / **Interim Summary**
instead of its `under_l1` guess; codability Discharge either way. Re-run
`imaging_final` only.

**5. Let an ambulatory surgery centre operative note be outpatient.** In
`encounter_canon.json`, remove Operative Note from `applies_to.always` (keep it in
`families`):

```json
"applies_to": {"families": ["Progress Note", "Discharge Summary", "Operative Note"],
               "always": {"Discharge Summary": "inpatient"}}
```

Effect on 6.4 encounter 2: the family no longer decides; the first page's own vote
(F2F, the negatives having cancelled Inpatient) decides → **Outpatient (F2F)**.
Operative notes without ASC wording keep Inpatient through their tier 1 page type.

**6. Teach the specialty keywords a practice name.** In `specialty_canon.json`, add
`"heart institute"` to Cardiology's `header` list (it is there already as an example of
the shape):

```json
"Cardiology": {"header": ["cardiology", "cardiovascular", "heart institute", "heart center", "cardiac center"],
               "body": ["echocardiogram", "electrocardiogram", "pacemaker", "ejection fraction", "..."]}
```

Effect: a page with that name in its top 18% of lines scores 6 for Cardiology and,
with a lead of 3, gets it whenever the model does not assert a specialty.

### 9.4 Which re-run an edit needs

The API always reprocesses; `only: [...]` runs the named stages on what is on disk
(`skip_ocr` avoids the billed Azure pass). Stages read each other's stored outputs, so
an edit needs its own stage **and every later stage that reads it**:

| You edited | Run `only:` |
|---|---|
| `page_keyword_weights.json`, `page_canon.json` (+ refit), `page_taxonomy.json` label space, `page_arbitration.json → axes / axes_rules`, `specialty_canon.json` | `["page_subtype", "dos_extract", "continuity", "encounter_type", "page_sequencing", "imaging_final"]` (DOS reads the Extracted subtype) |
| `continuity_canon.json` | `["continuity", "encounter_type", "page_sequencing", "imaging_final"]` |
| `encounter_canon.json` | `["encounter_type", "imaging_final"]` |
| `page_arbitration.json → final_placement / document_agreement / last_page_documents / inside_progress_note`; `page_taxonomy.json → codeability` (Final) | `["imaging_final"]` |
| `section_header_canon.json` | `["section_headers"]`, then the row for `page_subtype` above |
| `confidence_canon.json` | every stage whose confidences you want refreshed |

```bash
curl -X POST localhost:8001/api/charts/run -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"input_type":"local","input_path":"/data/inbox","chart_name":"<chart>",
       "only":["continuity","encounter_type","page_sequencing","imaging_final"]}'
```

### 9.5 Per-chart staging JSON

All under `<DATA_ROOT>/<chart>/staging/`, written atomically (a temp file, then
replace). Values below are invented, in the real files' shapes.

**`page_classification.json`** (`page_classify/stage.write_staged`, version 1): one
entry per page name, the Extracted answer and the whole `axes`:

```json
{"version": 1, "chart_name": "<chart>", "updated_at": "2026-10-28T09:12:44+00:00",
 "pages": {
  "3.jpg": {
   "page_type": "Lab Report (Lab Result)", "page_subtype": "Chemistry Report",
   "model_type": "Lab Report > Lab Result > Chemistry Report",
   "classification_category": "non_codeable", "confidence": 0.8201,
   "decided_by": "model", "needs_review": false, "ocr_source": "final2",
   "model_paths": [{"rank": 1, "path": "Lab Report > Lab Result > Chemistry Report",
                    "l1": "Lab Report", "l1_confidence": 0.86, "l2": "Lab Result",
                    "l2_confidence": 0.9, "l3": "Chemistry Report", "l3_confidence": 0.62,
                    "score": 0.4799}],
   "axes": {
    "route": "model", "decided_by": "model",
    "l1": "Lab Report", "l1_confidence": 0.8808, "l2": "Lab Result", "l2_confidence": 0.9311,
    "l3": "Chemistry Report", "l3_confidence": 0.6776, "page_type_confidence": 0.8201,
    "specialty": "General", "specialty_confidence": 0.546,
    "codeability": "non_codeable", "codeability_confidence": 0.8808,
    "sources": "model/model/model/others/canon",
    "canon_page_type": "Chemistry Report", "codeability_rule": "page_type_tag",
    "title_l1": null, "taxonomy_version": "v29.2", "rules": null,
    "paths": [{"rank": 1, "path": "Lab Report > Lab Result > Chemistry Report", "l1": "Lab Report",
               "l2": "Lab Result", "l3": "Chemistry Report", "score": 0.3166},
              {"rank": 4, "path": "Progress Note > Lab and Imaging Results", "l1": "Progress Note",
               "l2": "Lab and Imaging Results", "l3": null, "score": 0.0149}],
    "under_l1": {"Progress Note": {"l2": "Lab and Imaging Results", "l2_confidence": 0.2227,
                                   "l2_source": "canon", "l3": null, "l3_confidence": null},
                 "Order and Requisition": {"l2": "Laboratory Requisition", "l2_confidence": 0.6729,
                                           "l2_source": "model", "l3": null, "l3_confidence": null}},
    "prediction": {"L1": "Lab Report", "L1_confidence": 0.86,
                   "L1_top": [["Lab Report", 0.86], ["Progress Note", 0.08], ["Order and Requisition", 0.03]],
                   "specialty_top": [["Unknown", 0.81], ["Pathology", 0.09], ["Nephrology", 0.04]]}}}}}
```

How it helps: `sources` says who decided each of L1 / L2 / L3 / specialty /
codeability (model, canon, rule, others); `rules` names a page rule; `under_l1` and
`paths` show what the page would be in another document, which is exactly what the
Final step uses (a reviewer asking "why is this lab page Progress Note / Lab and
Imaging Results?" finds `under_l1["Progress Note"]` here and the document in
`continuity.json`); `prediction` is the model's raw answer, to tell a model error from
a keyword or rule error. Postgres `page_classification.axes` holds the same `axes`
object, and that is what later stages read: **this file is written and read by
nothing today** (S11).

**`continuity.json`** (`continuity/stage._write_scores`, version 2) — the documents,
each gap in words, and the encounters:

```json
{"version": 2, "chart_name": "<chart>", "written_at": "2026-10-28T09:13:02+00:00",
 "documents": [
  {"document": 1, "kind": "Document", "page_type": "Progress Note", "dos": "2024-03-21", "pages": ["1.jpg", "2.jpg"]},
  {"document": 2, "kind": "Document", "page_type": "Lab Report", "dos": "2024-03-14", "pages": ["3.jpg", "4.jpg", "5.jpg"]}],
 "pages": {
  "3.jpg": {"page": 3, "kind": "main",
   "prev_to_this": {"decision": "Break", "score": 12.81, "sure": "high",
                    "because": ["previous page signed at its end +3", "previous page is its last numbered page +3",
                                "different DOS +3", "page type changes +2.02"]},
   "this_to_next": {"decision": "Same document", "score": -13.6, "sure": "high",
                    "because": ["page numbers run on -5", "same DOS -3", "same header line -2", "wording runs on -1.33"]},
   "final": {"document": 2, "tag": "New (Doc 2)", "page_type": "Lab Report", "dos": "2024-03-14", "review": false}}},
 "encounters": [
  {"seq": 2, "document": 2, "page_family": "Lab Report", "pages": ["3.jpg", "4.jpg"],
   "dos_from": "2024-03-14", "dos_to": "2024-03-14", "encounter_type": "not_applicable",
   "encounter_label": "Not Available", "confidence": 0.975, "decided_by": "not_applicable",
   "reason": "Lab Report: encounter type does not apply", "matched_keyword": "Lab Report",
   "conflict": false, "contenders": []}]}
```

`because` lists the four strongest signals in words, after any rule that forced the
gap. review-ui's Local Mode reads `encounters` (`services/staged_continuity.py`); in
Production Mode the same grouping comes from `encounter_type_results.encounter_seq`.
`final.page_type` is continuity's carried value (the first page's Extracted L1), not the
Final page type.

**`continuity_detail.json`** — every fact and every signal, for debugging a wrong break:

```json
{"page_name": "3.jpg", "page_number": 3, "kind": "main", "document": 2, "label": "New (Doc 2)",
 "tag": "new", "break_before": 12.81, "break_after": -13.6, "decided_by": "sum",
 "confidence": 0.7974, "confidence_level": "medium", "review_required": false,
 "dos_from": "2024-03-14", "dos_trust": 3, "printed": [1, 2], "signed": false,
 "signed_in_tail": false, "title": false, "page_type": "Lab Report", "visit_ids": [],
 "gap_before": {"score": 12.81, "decision": "break", "decided_by": "sum", "confidence": 0.7974,
   "level": "high",
   "ending": {"signed_in_tail": 3.0, "signed_anywhere": 2.0, "last_numbered_page": 3.0,
              "page_fill": 2.0, "cut_mid_sentence": -1.0},
   "opening": {"starts_mid_thought": -1.0},
   "pair": {"different_dos": 3.0, "page_type_change": 2.02, "wording_char": -0.15, "wording_word": -0.06},
   "sub_scores": {"ending": 9.0, "opening": -1.0, "pair": 4.81},
   "recorded": {"text_start": -0.67}, "flags": [], "final_pass": null, "evidence": {}}}
```

Reading it: `dos_trust` 3 explains why the date change was not decisive; `sub_scores`
with one negative side explains the confidence of 0.80 on a +12.8 break (4.10);
`final_pass` names a merge or split that reversed the score; `recorded` holds what was
computed but not allowed to count (text_start, a conflict pattern).

**`blank_junk.json`** (stage 3 / 7): per page `pass_1`, `pass_2` and `final`
(decision, rule R1–R6, why, the DistilBERT `p_junk` / `p_keep`, duplicate status and
score). Continuity reads the final verdict through Postgres; review-ui reads the
duplicate scores from this file.

**`extraction.json`** (stage 56, key/value extraction): per page `fields` → rows
(`key`, `region` header / body / footer, `sentence`, `value`, `score`, `model_score`,
`source` model / rules / ner, `accepted`, `selected`, `tier` for DOS) and `absent`
(the confidence that a field is truly absent). **Continuity reads it first** for DOS
trust (the tier of the row that read the page's date), the printed page number, the
e-signatures (and whether one is in the footer), visit IDs and section headings
(`continuity/stage._staged_*`).

**Re-runs with `only`.** Because every stage reads the stored outputs of the earlier
ones (Postgres, plus these files for continuity), a canon edit is applied to an
existing chart by re-running from the stage that reads the edited file (9.4); the
OCR and its bill are not repeated.

### 9.6 Training JSON: from labels and ground truth to a model

```
 page images + image_labels.csv
     │  annotation tool (training/annotation-tool): tag L1 / L2 / L3 / specialty, Save,
     │  "Add to Training JSON" (Tesseract text of the page)
     ▼
 training_data.jsonl  (one page per line)
     │  utilities/merge_training_json.py  (several folders → one file)   ← fixed 2026-10-28 (kept v29.2 records)
     ▼
 training/bert-training/data/training_data.jsonl
     │  train_axes.py / page_axes.ipynb (Colab GPU): split by chart, small classes oversampled
     ▼
 output/page-axes/: encoder/, heads.pt, label_space.json, metrics_summary.json, split.csv
     │  copy to core-pipeline/models/page-family/page-axes/ (weights by scp, not git)
     │  keyword_vote fit --data training_data.jsonl --split split.csv   (refit the weights)
     │  keyword_vote evaluate ... --model ... --save                    (re-tune share / full)
     ▼
 the pipeline
```

**A training record** (`training/annotation-tool/store.py`, `_record_values`):

```json
{"image": "C0042_p007.jpg", "text": "NORTHFIELD REGIONAL LABORATORY ...",
 "L1": "Lab Report", "L2": "Lab Result", "L3": "Chemistry Report",
 "path": "Lab Report > Lab Result > Chemistry Report", "specialty": "Unknown",
 "codeability": "non_codeable", "codeability_rule": "page_type_tag",
 "chart_id": "C0042", "page_index": "7", "n_chars": 1184, "ocr_engine": "tesseract",
 "file_size": 412233}
```

`train_axes.py` reads `text` and the labels; classes come from the taxonomy and the
data (an L1 trains with ≥ 11 pages, an L2 / L3 / specialty with ≥ 3; rarer ones fold
to OTHER / Others); `label_space.json` records the classes, `taxonomy_version`,
`max_length` 512, `head_fraction` 0.85 and `train_pages` per class (which the
specialty gate reads). `metrics_summary.json` holds the test scores (section 11).

**The encounter record** (`encounter_tags.jsonl`): `text`, `encounter_type` (the
visit's), `encounter_on_page` (the setting only when the page shows it, else "No
evidence on page"), `basis` (stated, field, inferred, prior, inherited, default),
`train` (false for client-default rows). Inherited labels train at weight 0.5; "No
evidence on page" rows are left out by default.

**The keyword weights are training output too.** `keyword_vote fit` needs the same
`training_data.jsonl` and the run's `split.csv`: the weights are fitted on the
training side only, the vote's share and full tuned on the validation side, the test
side reported. That is why a canon edit needs a refit (9.3, edit 2).

**Ground truth today.** Client sheets and reviewer edits are stored in
`page_ground_truth` (one row per chart page: page type, subtype, codeable, DOS,
encounter, sequence, provider specialty ...) and `page_ground_truth_entry` (each
bulk upload or manual entry with its `labels` JSONB and `user_id`); review-ui compares
the pipeline's values against them (`docs/gt_page_type_mapping.csv` maps the client's
page-type names to the taxonomy). `encounter_review` holds a reviewer's encounter
ranges. **Nothing turns these into training data**: no code reads them in `training/`
or `utilities/`. Every reviewer correction is a labelled page, a labelled document
boundary and a labelled encounter that the models never see (S4).

**[fixed 2026-10-28] `merge_training_json.py`** expected the pre-v29.2 record
(`model_type`, `page_type`, `page_subtype`), so every v29.2 record was dropped (tested:
two records in, 0 written). It now keeps a record with `image`, `text` and either `L1`
or `model_type`, counts a changed L1 / L2 / L3 / specialty / codeability as a label
change, and checks L1s against `training/bert-training/taxonomy.json`.

---
## 10. Suggestions to improve the technique

Each suggestion names the weakness it answers (seen in the code or in a measured
number), the change, the expected benefit, the cost, and how to measure it. Priority:
**P1** cheap and it removes a known wrong answer; **P2** clear gain, moderate work;
**P3** larger projects.

### 10.1 How to measure anything here

Most suggestions need the same yardstick, so build it once:

| Decision | Ground truth available | Metric |
|---|---|---|
| Final page type, codability | `page_ground_truth.page_type` / `page_subtype` / `codeable` (client sheets), `page_ground_truth_entry` (reviewer edits), mapped with `docs/gt_page_type_mapping.csv` | per-page accuracy and macro F1 of L1 and codability; the confusion Lab Report ↔ Progress Note both ways; Codable precision (a Non-Codable page billed as Codable is the costly error) |
| Document boundaries | `encounter_review` (reviewer page ranges); `page_ground_truth` DOS + encounter label (a change of DOS or encounter = a boundary); the 170 pagination-labelled gaps; the training set's 397 visits (`encounter_tags.jsonl`) | per gap: precision / recall of breaks, balanced accuracy; per document: exact-match rate and the boundary F1 at ±0 pages; always GroupKFold by chart |
| Encounter | `page_ground_truth.encounter_label`, `encounter_tags.jsonl` | per page and per visit, recall per class (Evisit and Home Visit above all) |
| Specialty | `page_ground_truth.provider_specialty` | accuracy where the truth is a specialty; the share left General |
| Confidence | all the above | reliability per module: bucket the stored confidences (0.1 wide), share right per bucket |

A small script that joins `imaging_final` to `page_ground_truth` per chart and prints
these is the first deliverable; review-ui's accuracy pages compute part of it already.

### 10.2 The list, by priority

| # | Suggestion | Priority | Benefit | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | Narrow `progress_note_runs_on` | P1 | removes Codable consents / forms swallowed by an unsigned note | small (one rule + tests) |
| S2 | Fix the continuity tie-breaker defects | P1 | honest gap confidences; no false break push on same-type pages | very small |
| S3 | Fit the continuity weights and curve on ground truth | P1 | measured weights instead of "assumed"; calibrated gap confidence | 2–4 days |
| S4 | Feed reviewer corrections back as training data (active learning) | P1 | the models learn the client's own pages; most gain per labelling hour | 3–5 days, then routine |
| S5 | Fix `merge_training_json.py` for v29.2 | P1 | the documented training flow works again | under a day |
| S6 | Let the ASC / same-day negatives beat `applies_to.always` | P1 | ENC_03-type operative notes read Outpatient | small |
| S7 | Decide the Pathology Report L2 codability | P1 | one consistent tag | a decision + one line |
| S8 | Normalise `paths`; then build `page_type_overlap` | P2 | a continuity signal that is not a coin toss on ambiguous pages | 1–2 days + measurement |
| S9 | A Progress Note L2 that is learned, not a keyword share | P2 | subtype named on more note pages (PN L2 0.276 today) | relabel / retrain cycle |
| S10 | Title zone by position, not line count | P2 | titles under a long letterhead count (28394399 p1 / p5) | small |
| S11 | Guard the canon → weights link | P2 | no silent no-op canon edits | small (a test) |
| S12 | Specialty from more than the first page; provider credentials | P2 | fewer General documents; specialty right when page 1 is generic | moderate |
| S13 | Handwritten pages | P2 | fewer Others / Unknown on handwritten notes | moderate |
| S14 | Calibrate every module's confidence | P2 | "80%" means right 8 times in 10 | 1–2 days once S3 / 10.1 exist |
| S15 | A learned boundary model over the gap signals | P3 | better boundaries where signals interact | 1–2 weeks |
| S16 | Layout fingerprint from the OCR boxes | P3 | template changes caught without words | 1 week |
| S17 | Encounter before continuity, and `encounter_change` | P3 | setting changes split documents | 3–5 days |
| S18 | Use or drop `staging/page_classification.json` | P3 | no write-only artefact | small |

### 10.3 Each suggestion

**S1. Narrow `progress_note_runs_on`.**
*Weakness:* the rule merges any non-decisive break after a Progress Note document whose
last page is unsigned — breaks of +7.8 and +6.4 in 4.10 — and the consent behind it
becomes Progress Note / Others, **Codable** (5.12). The trigger is the absence of one
signature line, which OCR often misses.
*Change:* merge only when the break is weak (`level` low or score ≤ `weak_score`, as
the other merges require), **and** the next document's first page is one of the
embedded families (Lab Report, Medication Record, Problem List, Forms and
Questionnaires, Order and Requisition) **or** its L1 is Progress Note, **and** it has
no title. Never absorb Consent and Authorization, Administrative, Demographics,
Provider Letter, Patient Communication.
*Benefit:* removes a class of false-Codable pages; keeps the intended case (a lab table
after a note).
*Cost:* a few lines in `engine._final_pass` + tests on the 4.10 chart.
*Measure:* on the ground-truth charts, Codable precision and boundary F1 before / after;
count of `merged: progress_note_runs_on` gaps per chart and how many the truth calls a
boundary.

**S2. Fix the continuity tie-breaker defects.** *(a) fixed 2026-10-28; (b) and (c) open.*
*Weakness:* (a) `starts_mid_thought` tests `islower()` on text already lower-cased by
`engine.normalize`, so it fires on every gap where `wording_char` ≥ 0.05; it makes the
opening sub-score negative and costs clean breaks the 0.8 `conflict` penalty (+12.8 →
0.80 instead of 0.997 in 4.10). (b) `wording_char` gates the two tie-breakers at a
character cosine of 0.0125 (0.05 × the 0.25 divisor) — almost any shared trigram. (c) `page_type_change`
fires on same-L1 pairs: 3 × (1 − c) × c, up to +0.75 at c = 0.5, a push toward a break
between two pages the classifier calls the same.
*Change:* keep the original casing in `Line` (or test the raw OCR line); gate the
tie-breakers on `wording_char` ≥ a measured value (e.g. 0.3); for the same L1 use −(c ×
points) or 0, not a positive push.
*Benefit:* confidences that mean what they say; fewer spurious "Continue?" flags.
*Cost:* an hour plus re-running the continuity tests.
*Measure:* on labelled gaps, the share of breaks with confidence ≥ 0.8 that are right,
before / after; no change expected in the decisions themselves except near 0.

**S3. Fit the continuity weights and curve on ground truth.**
*Weakness:* 8 of the 24 signals are "assumed" in the canon (including the decisive
date weights and `page_type_change`) and 4 more are unmeasured, user-set or "deliberately weak"; the measured ones came from 170 gaps over 15
charts with standalone re-implementations of the extractors (canon `measurement`:
balanced accuracy 0.79 ± 0.19), and `decision.a` / `b` were never fitted.
*Change:* export every gap of every ground-truth chart from `continuity_detail.json`
(each signal's raw value, not its points), label it break / join from 10.1, and fit an
L2-regularised logistic regression, GroupKFold by chart. Use the coefficients (scaled
so the strongest equals 5) as the new `points`, the intercept and scale as `b` and `a`.
Keep the user rules (Demographics, visit heading, Visual) as rules, and report how
often each fires against the truth.
*Benefit:* weights that reflect this client's charts; a gap confidence that is a real
probability; signals that do not help get 0 (a signal firing on joins as often as on
breaks).
*Cost:* 2–4 days, most of it building the labelled gap set.
*Measure:* boundary F1 and balanced accuracy, cross-validated by chart, against the
current canon; a reliability plot of the new confidence.

**S4. Feed reviewer corrections back as training data (active learning).**
*Weakness:* `page_ground_truth_entry` stores every reviewer's corrected page type,
codability, DOS and encounter, but nothing in `training/` or `utilities/` reads it;
the models were trained on 2,785 annotation-tool pages (Tesseract text) and never see
the client's corrections. Small classes are the weak ones (Problem List L1 F1 0,
Order and Requisition 0.20; Evisit and Home Visit recall 0.33–0.60).
*Change:* a script that turns a chart's ground truth into `training_data.jsonl` rows
(the page's stored OCR text from `ocr_results`, the corrected L1 / L2 / L3 mapped
through `gt_page_type_mapping.csv`, `chart_id` for the split) and encounter rows for
`encounter_tags.jsonl`. Then choose what to review next by **uncertainty**: pages
whose Final page type confidence is under 0.5, whose `paths` top two are close, or
where model and keywords disagreed (`sources` canon at L1 against a confident model);
review-ui can sort by those.
*Benefit:* the biggest expected accuracy gain per labelling hour, on the client's own
templates and OCR; small classes grow where they matter.
*Cost:* 3–5 days for the export and a review queue; then a retrain on Colab per batch.
*Measure:* held-out charts (never in training) before / after each retrain: L1 macro F1,
small-class recall, Codable precision; the number of corrections per 100 pages over
time.

**S5. Fix `merge_training_json.py` for v29.2.** *Done 2026-10-28.* *Weakness:* it requires `model_type`
and drops every v29.2 record (9.6). *Change:* require `image`, `text`, `L1`; compare
labels on `L1`, `L2`, `L3`, `specialty`; validate paths against `label_space`.
*Cost:* hours. *Measure:* merging two real annotation folders writes every page once.

**S6. Let the ASC / same-day negatives beat `applies_to.always`.** *Weakness:* the
negatives were added for ENC_03 but `family_decision` runs first (6.4). *Change:* in
`family_decision`, skip `always` when a `tier1: true` negative cancelling that setting
fired on the encounter's pages (fall through to the first page's vote). *Cost:* small.
*Measure:* the 22 encounter test pages (ENC_03) and operative notes in ground truth.

**S7. Decide the Pathology Report L2 codability.** `Lab Report > Pathology Report` is
codeable while all its L3s are non_codeable (5.6). Either tag the L2 non_codeable
(consistent with its L3s) or tag the L3s that should be Codable. One line in
`page_taxonomy.json` (and the training taxonomy).

**S8. Normalise `paths`; then build `page_type_overlap`.** *Weakness:* the path score
multiplies votes and counts a missing level as 1.0, so shallow paths outrank deep ones
(3.7); and `page_type_change` uses only the top-1 L1, so an ambiguous page (Progress
Note 0.45 / Lab Report 0.40) flips +3 on a coin toss. *Change:* rank paths by the
geometric mean of their levels' votes (or by the model's path probability, which is
normalised), and use the L1 marginals of `axes.paths` for the overlap signal of 4.12.
*Measure:* with S3's labelled gaps, the signal's firing rate on breaks vs joins and the
balanced accuracy with / without it.

**S9. A Progress Note L2 that is learned.** *Weakness:* the Progress Note L2 is the
keyword share alone; a full visit note spreads its words over many sections and stays
Others (example C); measured Progress Note L2 accuracy 0.276 on test pages. *Change:*
either (a) train the L2 head under Progress Note (labels exist for split pages), or (b)
a rule: the section with the most body text on the page (from the OCR section headers'
positions) is the L2, and "Others" only when no section heading is found. *Measure:*
Progress Note L2 accuracy on test pages and ground truth.

**S10. Title zone by position.** *Weakness:* the title zone is the first 12 non-empty
lines; under a long letterhead the title sits on line 18–23 and counts as body
(28394399 p1 / p5 read Medication Record). *Change:* with line positions (Final 2 /
Final 1), the title zone is the top 45% of the page height, or the first lines after
the letterhead block; the line count stays the fallback. *Measure:* the two known pages
and the Lab Report ↔ Progress Note confusion on test pages.

**S11. Guard the canon → weights link.** *Weakness:* a canon edit is a silent no-op for
the vote until `fit` is re-run (9.3). *Change:* a test that every `strong_terms` entry
of `page_canon.json` appears in `page_keyword_weights.json → strong`, and that the
weights file is newer than the canon; `/health` could report "keyword weights stale".
*Measure:* the test fails on a canon edit without a refit.

**S12. Specialty from more than the first page.** *Weakness:* Final specialty is the
first page's only; a generic first page gives General to a whole cardiology document;
`inherit_across_document` in the canon is not honoured; provider credentials and names
(extracted per page) are unused; the model asserts a specialty on about 30% of test
pages (119 of 397). *Change:* the document's specialty = the first page's when it is
not General, else the most confident specialty among the document's pages (model or
letterhead keywords), else one implied by the signing provider's credentials (DPM →
Podiatry, OD → Ophthalmology, CRNA → Anesthesia, DC → Rehabilitation ...) or an NPI
taxonomy lookup. *Measure:* `provider_specialty` in ground truth: accuracy and the
share of General.

**S13. Handwritten pages.** *Weakness:* a handwritten page never uses Tesseract text;
without Final OCR it has no text and no page type (`no_text`); with Azure text the
model sees OCR it was not trained on (training text is Tesseract); only a 0.9
confidence penalty marks it. *Change:* (a) always run Final 2 on handwritten pages
(already the case when Azure is configured: check `/health`); (b) train with Final 2
text for handwritten pages (re-OCR the training pages with Azure, or at least the
handwritten ones); (c) in continuity, down-weight text signals (wording, sections) on
handwritten pages and lean on dates and page numbers. *Measure:* L1 accuracy and Others
rate on handwritten ground-truth pages, before / after.

**S14. Calibrate every module's confidence.** *Weakness:* `calibration` is empty; the
penalties are first settings. *Change:* with 10.1's reliability data, fit
piecewise-linear knots per module (isotonic regression, then thinned) into
`confidence_canon.json → calibration`. *Measure:* expected calibration error per module
before / after; the share of review flags that were real errors.

**S15. A learned boundary model.** Once S3 has a labelled gap set: a gradient-boosted
classifier (or a CRF over the gap sequence, so one confident boundary informs its
neighbours) on the raw signal values plus page-type marginals, layout and encounter
features. Keep the rules and the decisive date / pagination signals as constraints.
*Benefit:* interactions a sum cannot express (a title counts less when the page
numbers run on). *Cost:* 1–2 weeks. *Measure:* boundary F1 by chart against S3's fitted
sum; adopt only if it wins on held-out charts.

**S16. Layout fingerprint from the OCR boxes** (the 4.12 design): header / footer zones
with masked digits and horizontal anchors, margins, line height, columns, page size.
*Measure:* as S8, per signal.

**S17. Encounter before continuity, and `encounter_change`** (4.12): split the page vote
from the encounter decision, run the vote at seq < 87. *Measure:* boundary F1 on
charts that mix inpatient and office documents.

**S18. Use or drop `staging/page_classification.json`.** It is written on every run
and read by nothing; either make it review-ui's Local Mode source for the Extracted
page type (with `paths` and `under_l1`, which review-ui does not show today) or stop
writing it.

---

## 11. Measurements to date

Kept from the 2026-10-25 edition (dates as stated; not re-measured for this one) and
from the installed models' `metrics_summary.json`.

**Page-axes model installed** (`models/page-family/page-axes/metrics_summary.json`;
Bio_ClinicalBERT, best epoch 10, 397 test pages):

| Head | Accuracy | Macro F1 |
|---|---|---|
| L1 | 0.743 | 0.580 |
| L2 (given the right L1; 212 pages) | 0.835 | 0.616 |
| L3 (given the right L2; 71 pages) | 0.831 | 0.750 |
| Specialty | 0.660 | 0.496 |
| Full path | 0.632 | hierarchical P 0.662, R 0.716, F1 0.688 |

Lab Report read as Progress Note: 1 of 84; Progress Note read as Lab Report: 10 of 181.
Routes: model 338, canon 51, review 8.

**The keyword vote with that model** (weights fitted on its 2,005 training pages,
share / full tuned on 382 validation pages, 397 test pages; L2 / L3 given the true
parent; 2026-10-25):

| Test pages | Model alone | Keywords alone | The vote |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | 0.741 | 0.259 | 0.741 |
| L2, true L1 (not Progress Note) | 0.819 | 0.671 | 0.815 |
| L3, true L2 | 0.817 | 0.479 | **0.859** |
| Progress Note L2 | 0.133 | 0.276 | **0.276** |
| whole path | 0.338 | 0.136 | **0.380** |
| Lab Report read as Progress Note | 1 / 84 | 0 / 84 | 1 / 84 |
| Progress Note read as Lab Report | 10 / 181 | 4 / 181 | 11 / 181 |

**Encoder comparison** (same split and seed, 397 test pages, 2026-10-25): L1 accuracy /
macro F1 — DistilBERT 0.733 / 0.496, RoBERTa 0.710 / 0.516, **Bio_ClinicalBERT 0.743 /
0.580** (chosen), ModernBERT stopped (best validation 0.469). Bio_ClinicalBERT costs
0.17 s a page on a laptop CPU (DistilBERT 0.08 s). Problem List (F1 0) and Order and
Requisition (0.20) stay weak with every encoder: they need pages. Specialty gate 0.5 /
0.1: the model asserts a specialty on 119 of 397 test pages and is right on 86%.

**Encounter model installed** (`models/encounter/metrics_summary.json`; DistilBERT, page
target, best epoch 9, 283 test pages): accuracy **0.760**, macro F1 0.637; recall
Inpatient 0.40, F2F 0.57, Evisit 0.60, Home Visit 0.33, Not Available 0.94; visits (51)
0.71. By label basis: stated 0.47, field 0.41, inferred 0.60, prior 0.94.
Bio_ClinicalBERT was tried for encounter and not taken (accuracy 0.746, macro F1 0.53).

**Encounter rules engine** against `encounter_tags.jsonl` (2,729 learnable pages, before
the model vote; 2026-10-25): page accuracy 0.813; recall F2F 0.80, Evisit **0.07**,
Home Visit **0.04**, Inpatient 0.62, Not Available 0.97. With the model vote, on the 22
encounter test pages: 19 of 21 (ENC_03 and ENC_09 wrong).

**Continuity** (`continuity_canon.json → measurement`, 2026-10-18): 985 pairs, 170
labelled by explicit pagination on both pages (129 continue, 41 new) over 15 charts;
cross-validated balanced accuracy **0.79 ± 0.19** (GroupKFold by chart). Known bias:
documents that print pagination are longer and more template-driven, so furniture
signals may be overstated and prose signals understated.

**Keyword canon** after the conversion to v29.2: its path agrees with the majority
label for 93 of the 106 canon page types that occur in the training data.

**Test-chart review** (2026-10-25; seven charts, 161 pages, no ground truth): flags after
that day's changes — page type 51, codability 49, encounter type 24, specialty 23,
handwriting 10, blank/junk 2.

## 12. Open decisions

* **[decide]** S1: which families `progress_note_runs_on` may absorb.
* **[decide]** S7: the codability of `Lab Report > Pathology Report`.
* **[decide]** Five old page-type names without an exact v29.2 home: Administration,
  Administrative Form, Policy → Administrative > Others; Pap and HPV / Pap smear report
  → Lab Report > Diagnostic Study today, Lab Report > Pathology Report > Cytology Report
  recommended.
* **[decide]** A page only the Others step decided reads "Others / Others": acceptable?
* **[decide]** More `final_placement` rows (e.g. Progress Note inside Discharge Summary,
  9.3 edit 4).
* **[decide]** The strong words: strike any word in `class_keywords.json → strong` that
  can head a page of another type ("authorization" pins Consent and Authorization; a
  Prior Authorization page is also Consent and Authorization, but an "Authorization"
  heading on a referral letter would be pinned wrongly).
* **[decide]** Titles below a letterhead (S10).
* **[decide]** Continuity before or after sequencing (7).
* **[decide]** The client default for outpatient encounters with no modality evidence
  (Outpatient Evisit) (6.5).
* **[decide]** Payer record-retrieval fax covers and EHR import covers as junk by rule;
  surgery scheduling forms as Administrative > Appointment Record; Records Request
  defined by purpose.
* **[decide]** The context rules not built (5.13).

## Appendix: the canon, taxonomy v29.2

Rendered from the JSON files by `training/taxonomy/render_canon_doc.py`; run it after any canon change.

Contents: A. label space · B. renames · C. context and MECE rules · D. canon conversion tables · E. keyword canon · F. specialty · G. sections · H. encounter (training) · I. encounter (pipeline) · J. continuity

### A. The v29.2 label space

Version **v29.2**. Counts: L1 17, L2 102, L3 64, specialty 34, paths 167.

#### A.1 Rules

* **section_L1**: Progress Note
* **section_L1_note**: Under Progress Note, L2 is the content section on the page (axis B), decided by section tagging. It is confirmed in the tool and not trained.
* **fallback**: Others
* **specialty_unknown**: Unknown
* **specialty_other**: Others
* **specialty_note**: 'Unknown' means no signal on the page; 'Others' means a real service line the list does not carry yet, the signal to extend the lexicon.
* **codeability_values**: codeable, non_codeable, discharge, needs_review
* **codeability_default**: needs_review
* **note_v29_2**: Labels follow Training/processed/taxonomy_v29.json context_rules (page_essence etc.). Section L1 (Progress Note) L2 is owned by section tagging at inference, as before.

#### A.2 L1

| L1 | Description |
|---|---|
| Progress Note | A clinician's record of an encounter. L2 here is the CONTENT SECTION the page carries, not the kind of note, because a scanned page of a note is a page and what matters is what is on it. A note is a Progress Note whatever service produced it; the service line goes on the specialty axis. |
| Lab Report | The result of a test, study or examination performed on the patient. The distinction from Operative Note is that a result reports on looking, measuring or analysing, while an Operative Note records an intervention. |
| Operative Note | The record of an operation or an episode of intensive care: what was done in theatre, by whom, and under what anaesthesia. Repeated treatments and office procedures are Procedure Note instead. |
| Procedure Note | A treatment RECORD: a repeated therapeutic session or an administration of something to the patient. Neither an operation, which is Operative Note, nor an encounter narrative, which is Progress Note. Dialysis is the clearest case: a run is a procedure performed on a schedule and logged on a flow sheet, not a visit written up. |
| Discharge Summary | The closing documentation of an inpatient or observation stay, including its instructions, medications and transfers of care. The client treats this whole group as its own codeability bucket rather than as codeable or non-codeable. |
| Clinical Summary | A document that summarises care across a visit or many visits: CCD / Summary of Care / Transition of Care (C-CDA), After-Visit Summary, EHR patient-summary or chart-snapshot printouts. Decided by the document title. |
| Consent and Authorization |  |
| Demographics | Patient identity, contact and coverage information, carrying no clinical content. |
| Immunization | A record of vaccines administered or due. |
| Medication Record | A list or administration record of what the patient is taking or has been given, including blood products. |
| Order and Requisition | An instruction to perform something: a test, a treatment, a referral. The order is not the result, which is why these never sit under Lab Report/Result. |
| Problem List | A standing list of the patient's active or historical problems. Flat by design: the page type is the whole answer and no L2 adds anything. |
| Forms and Questionnaires | A structured form completed by or about the patient. Codeability depends on context: bound into a progress note it is part of that note, standing alone it is not. |
| Provider Letter | A letter between providers or to the practice about the patient: referral letters, consultation letters, records requests (defined by purpose: any letter, fax or EHR note requesting records). |
| Patient Communication | Correspondence between the practice and the patient or another provider, and material sent to the patient. |
| Administrative | Pages that exist for the running of the practice rather than the care of the patient: scheduling, billing, coding, records management. |
| Others | A page nothing else claims. This is a routing decision, not a classification: the page goes to an auditor rather than being forced into a bucket or dropped. |

#### A.3 L2 by L1

**Progress Note**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| CC/HPI | Codeable |  | The chief complaint and the history of present illness: why the patient came and the narrative of the problem. Usually the opening page of a note. |
| Visit Diagnosis | Codeable |  | The diagnosis or assessment arrived at for this encounter. Outranks every other section on a page, because a page that states the diagnosis is about the visit even if most of it is a data table. |
| Assessment and Plan | Codeable |  | The clinician's reasoning and what they intend to do next. Distinguished from Visit Diagnosis by being forward-looking rather than a statement of findings. |
| Summary | Codeable |  | A narrative recap of the encounter or stay, including hospital course where it appears inside a note. |
| Problem List | Non-Codeable |  | A problem list printed as a section inside a note, as opposed to a standalone problem list page, which is its own L1. |
| Medication List | Non-Codeable |  | Medications and allergies as a section inside a note. A standalone medication page is Medication Record instead. |
| Lab and Imaging Results | Codeable |  | A split page of a note that carries results AND note content (history, exam, plan). A page that is wholly results is Lab Report, not this (page-essence rule). |
| Procedure List | Codeable |  | A procedure described inside a note, typically done in the office. A formal operative report is Operative Note. |
| Vitals | Needs Review |  | A note page dominated by vital signs. |
| Physical Exam | Codeable |  | Examination findings, review of systems and vital signs. |
| Questionnaire | Needs Review |  | A form or questionnaire bound into a note, which makes it part of that note. The same form standing alone is Forms and Questionnaires. |
| Patient Instructions | Codeable |  | What the patient was told to do, and follow-up arrangements. |
| Signature | Codeable |  | A page carrying the attestation and little else, typically the closing page of a note. The same evidence is the End-of-document signal used to group pages into documents. |
| Others | Codeable |  | A progress note page whose section could not be determined. Most often a continuation page of body text with no header on it. |

**Lab Report**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lab Result | Non-Codeable |  | Specimen-based testing: chemistry, haematology, microbiology, urinalysis, genetics, coagulation. |
| Radiology Report | Non-Codeable |  | Imaging reports: plain film, CT, MRI, ultrasound, mammography, nuclear medicine, bone density. Angiography is held separately from plain CT and MR because it is a different protocol and a different code. |
| Pathology Report | Codeable |  | Tissue and cell examination: biopsy, cytology, flow cytometry, molecular diagnostics. |
| Diagnostic Study | Non-Codeable |  | Any other study that produces a report: cardiac, neurological, pulmonary, sleep, vascular, endoscopic and ophthalmic. Deliberately broad. It holds many service lines at once, which is why no single specialty is implied at this level and the specialty is read from the page instead. |

**Operative Note**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Surgery Report | Codeable |  | The operative report itself: the account of an operation, its findings and its closure. |
| Procedure List | Codeable |  | A procedure page belonging to an operation: the intraoperative record, phase record or OR summary. Distinguished from the Procedure Note L1 not by name, which is the same, but by the document the page is bound into. Standing alone it is a treatment record and belongs under Procedure Note. |
| Pre-operative | Codeable |  | Pre-operative. |
| Post-operative | Needs Review |  | Post-operative. |
| Anesthesia Record | Codeable |  | The anaesthesia record and post-anaesthesia care, including the transfer of care out of theatre. |
| Critical Care Record | Codeable |  | Intensive care documentation: ICU flow sheets and ventilator management records. |
| Triage Report | Codeable |  | The triage assessment taken at presentation, carrying acuity and arrival details. |
| Intra-operative Record | Needs Review |  | Intra-operative nursing / OR record: times, staff, positioning, counts. |
| Implant Record | Needs Review |  | Implant log or sticker page: devices, lot numbers. |
| Others | Codeable |  | An operative page that fits none of the above, such as an implant log. |

**Procedure Note**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dialysis Record | Codeable |  | A dialysis run: treatment record, haemodialysis flow sheet or peritoneal dialysis record. Logged per session rather than written up per visit. |
| Infusion and Chemotherapy | Codeable |  | Administration of an infusion or chemotherapy, including the cycle and the agents given. |
| Radiation Therapy | Codeable |  | A radiation therapy treatment record: fields, fractions and dose delivered. |
| Wound Care | Codeable |  | Wound and burn treatment: measurements, debridement and dressing changes, recorded per session. |
| Therapy Session | Codeable |  | A therapy treatment session: physical, occupational, speech, respiratory, cardiac rehabilitation, chiropractic or acupuncture. The therapy DISCHARGE summary is Discharge Summary instead. |
| Elastography | Codeable |  | An elastography or FibroScan procedure measuring tissue stiffness. |
| Injection Visit | Codeable |  | A visit whose purpose is an injection, with the agent and site recorded. |
| Knee Procedure | Codeable |  | A procedure performed on the knee: injection, aspiration or arthrocentesis, written up on its own. The site is the L2 because it is what the code turns on. |
| Laser Procedure | Codeable |  | A laser treatment, including YAG and SLT. |
| Lumbar Procedure | Codeable |  | A procedure performed on the lumbar spine: injection, aspiration or arthrocentesis, written up on its own. The site is the L2 because it is what the code turns on. |
| Office Procedure | Codeable |  | A procedure performed in the office where no more specific type is identified, including gastroenterology and retinal office procedures. |
| Pathology Procedure | Codeable |  | A procedure carried out in the pathology laboratory, such as gross examination. |
| Endoscopy (EGD) | Codeable |  | Endoscopy procedure record (EGD) when kept as a procedure note rather than a report. |
| Cardiac Procedure | Codeable |  | Cardiac procedure record (cardioversion, cath-lab procedure log) that is not a study report. |
| Others | Codeable |  | A treatment record or procedure page with no specific procedure identified. A page headed only 'Procedure Note' with no site or modality lands here, because that is the label an unsure annotator reaches for. |

**Discharge Summary**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Depart/Discharge Note | Discharge |  | The discharge summary itself, in any of its names: depart note, depart summary, discharge note, discharge report, final case summary. |
| Discharge Instruction | Discharge |  | What the patient was told to do after discharge, including the Spanish-language versions of the same form. |
| Discharge Medication | Discharge |  | The medication list as it stands at discharge. |
| Transfer of Care | Discharge |  | Handover to another facility or service, including anaesthesia transfer of care. |
| Interim Summary | Discharge |  | A summary issued mid-stay rather than at discharge. |
| Short Stay Summary | Discharge |  | The discharge document for a short stay or observation episode. Its own header governs even where it embeds a history and physical. |
| Discharge Diagnosis | Discharge |  | Discharge Diagnosis. |
| History and Physical | Discharge |  | Admission H&P of an inpatient, observation or short stay. Office H&Ps are Progress Note. |
| Consultation Note | Discharge |  | Inpatient consult. Office consults are Progress Note. |
| Others | Discharge |  | A discharge-packet page that fits none of the above. |

**Clinical Summary**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Summary of Care (C-CDA) | Non-Codeable |  | CCD, Summary of Care, Transition of Care, C-CDA documents. |
| After-Visit Summary | Non-Codeable |  | After Visit Summary / Patient Clinical Summary given at the end of a visit. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | Others. |

**Consent and Authorization**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Consent for Treatment | Non-Codeable |  | Consent to treatment / conditions of treatment signed by the patient. |
| Surgical Consent | Needs Review |  | Surgical Consent. |
| Transfusion Consent | Needs Review |  | Transfusion Consent. |
| Immunization Consent | Needs Review |  | Immunization Consent. |
| HIPAA and Privacy | Non-Codeable |  | HIPAA and Privacy. |
| Release of Information | Needs Review |  | Release of Information. |
| Assignment of Benefits | Non-Codeable |  | The patient assigning insurance benefits to the provider. |
| General Consent | Non-Codeable |  | General Consent. |
| Prior Authorization | Needs Review |  | Prior Authorization. |
| Referral Authorization | Non-Codeable |  | Approval of a referral, with the visits authorised. |
| General Authorization | Non-Codeable |  | General Authorization. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | An administrative page that fits none of the above. |

**Demographics**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Patient Information | Codeable |  | The patient's identity, contact and coverage details. |
| Face Sheet | Codeable |  | The printed face sheet or registration page. Note that this overlaps Patient Demographics by design: both were asked for, and a face sheet is in practice the demographics page under another name. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | A demographic page that fits neither of the above. |

**Immunization**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Immunization Record | Non-Codeable |  | Vaccines given or due, in any format: immunisation list, vaccination administration record, flu shot record. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | An immunisation page that fits none of the above. |

**Medication Record**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Medication List | Non-Codeable |  | The current medication list as a standalone page. |
| Prescription Page | Non-Codeable |  | A prescription or Rx page. |
| Refill Record | Non-Codeable |  | A record of refills issued. |
| Blood Transfusion Record | Non-Codeable |  | Administration of blood or blood products, which is a medication administration rather than a procedure. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | A medication page that fits none of the above. |

**Order and Requisition**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Physician Order | Non-Codeable |  | Any order written by a clinician: medication, treatment, service request, post-operative orders. |
| Laboratory Requisition | Non-Codeable |  | A requisition or lab form requesting a test. |
| Referral Order | Needs Review |  | Referral Order. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | An order or request that fits neither of the above. |

**Problem List**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Problem List | Non-Codeable |  | A standing problem list page. |
| Diagnosis List/Index | Needs Review |  | A list or index of diagnoses, often coded, with no assessment. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | Others. |

**Forms and Questionnaires**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Assessment | Needs Review |  | A structured assessment or evaluation: functional status, ADL and IADL, nutrition, occupational health, fitness for duty, annual wellness. |
| Questionnaire | Needs Review |  | A questionnaire, checklist or screening instrument completed about the patient: screening tests, preventive checklists, health maintenance, Mini-Cog and clock drawing, growth charts. |
| Others | Needs Review |  | A form that is neither clearly a questionnaire nor clearly an assessment. |

**Provider Letter**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Referral Letter | Non-Codeable |  | Letter referring the patient to another provider. |
| Consultation Letter | Non-Codeable |  | Consultant letter back to the referring provider. |
| Records Request | Non-Codeable |  | Any letter, fax or EHR note whose purpose is requesting records. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | Others. |

**Patient Communication**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Patient Message | Non-Codeable |  | A message exchanged with the patient: telephone message, portal communication, web encounter. |
| Notice | Non-Codeable |  | A notice or notification sent to the patient, including discharge notices. |
| Patient Education Material | Non-Codeable |  | Educational material and patient instructions given out as their own document. |
| Telephone Encounter | Non-Codeable |  | Phone note, refill call, telephone message. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | A communication page that fits none of the above. |

**Administrative**

| L2 | Codeability | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Appointment Record | Non-Codeable |  | Appointment notes and summaries: when the patient was or is due to be seen. |
| Billing & Invoices | Needs Review |  | Invoices, billing pages, superbills, charge sheets and estimates. Codeability here can depend on the ingest source rather than the page. |
| Patient Registration | Needs Review |  | Registration / intake demographics form for registration. |
| Superbill | Non-Codeable |  | Encounter form / superbill with ICD-10 and CPT codes. Invoices and statements stay Billing & Invoices. |
| Others | Non-Codeable |  | An administrative page that fits none of the above. |

#### A.4 L3 (Lab Report only)

**Lab Report > Lab Result**

| L3 | Specialty | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Allergy Testing Report |  | Allergy Testing Report. Specimen-based testing: chemistry, haematology, microbiology, urinalysis, genetics, coagulation. |
| Chemistry Report |  | Chemistry Report. Specimen-based testing: chemistry, haematology, microbiology, urinalysis, genetics, coagulation. |
