| Operative Note | Procedure Note | Intra-operative Record |

#### K.4 document_agreement

Across a continuity document every page takes the first page's L1 (sub-types stay per page). Only when the first page is Unknown or Others (neither the model nor the canon was sure), a vote of the pages' L1s weighted by page-type confidence decides, Others pages left out. The vote counts only when one L1 holds at least vote_min_share of the weight; below that the pages keep their own types and are flagged for review (no_agreement). An Others page then takes the document's L1 with the L2 the page classifier gave it there (under_l1), else <L1> / Others.

`vote_min_share`: 0.6

#### K.5 last_page_documents

2026-10-25 (user): a short packet whose LAST page is one of these L1s takes that L1 on every page (a consent: notice / intake pages, then the signed consent page). Post-processing for the Final page type only (DOS and encounter keep continuity's documents): 1. a document whose last page is a listed L1 absorbs the documents right before it while the packet stays within max_pages and their pages are all of the joins L1s; 2. in a document of at most max_pages pages ending in a listed L1, every page takes that L1 (continuation_rule agreed_last_page); its L2 is the one the page classifier gave it there (under_l1), else its own name when that L1 has it, else Others.

| Family | max_pages | joins |
|---|---|---|
| Consent and Authorization | 4 | Consent and Authorization, Administrative, Demographics, Forms and Questionnaires |

#### K.6 inside_progress_note

Final rules (imaging_final, arbitration.decide_final). A page whose Final family becomes the section L1 (Progress Note) -- continuing a Progress Note document, or an embedded_families page headed by a Progress Note title -- takes its Final L2 from final_placement['Progress Note'][its own L1] (Lab Report -> Lab and Imaging Results), else Others. Its codability is its Final type's tag like every page (2026-10-25, user: no exception for lab or medication pages). non_codeable_names is no longer read.

`embedded_families`: Lab Report, Medication Record, Immunization, Problem List, Order and Requisition

`non_codeable_names` (no longer read): Lab Result, Medication Record, Medication List

#### K.7 confidence (the keyword canon's own)

The keyword canon's own confidence (axes canon_min / l3_canon_min compare against it): keyword_title_base for a title hit, else keyword_body_base, plus keyword_per_margin per point of margin over the next candidate, capped at keyword_title_cap / keyword_body_cap.

| Key | Value |
|---|---|
| `keyword_title_base` | 0.6 |
| `keyword_body_base` | 0.3 |
| `keyword_per_margin` | 0.05 |
| `keyword_title_cap` | 0.9 |
| `keyword_body_cap` | 0.6 |

`decided_by_values`: model, canon, rule, others, blank_junk, duplicate_of:<page id>, no_text

### N. Confidence settings (keyword-canon/confidence_canon.json)

2026-10-25 (user): one confidence scale for every decided value, so a reviewer can trust the number the same way everywhere: >= 0.80 good, 0.50-0.80 passable, < 0.50 bad -> flagged for manual review. A confidence is never a fixed constant: it starts from the model's probability for the value it decided (or the rule's own strength where no model exists), backing keywords lift it, and each reason for doubt multiplies it by a penalty below. stages/lib/confidence.py applies this file; docs/APPROACH.md section 10 explains it per module.

| Band | From |
|---|---|
| good | 0.8 |
| review below | 0.5 |

`agree_lift`: 0.5. Keywords or rules that back the same value lift the model's probability p toward 1: p + (1 - p) x agree_lift x support, support in [0, 1] (a strong title term 1.0, a weak body hint near 0). A model at 0.6 backed by a strong keyword reads 0.8; with no keyword it stays 0.6.

| Penalty | × |
|---|---|
| `keywords_disagree` | 0.7 |
| `no_model` | 0.85 |
| `fallback` | 0.6 |
| `conflict` | 0.8 |
| `overturned` | 0.7 |
| `handwritten` | 0.9 |
| `thin_text` | 0.85 |
| `degraded` | 0.6 |

keywords_disagree: the keywords / rules point to a different value than the one decided. no_model: a rule decided with no model to back it (or the model was unavailable). fallback: nothing decided it; a default filled it (Others, General, family default F2F, scan order kept with nothing to confirm it). conflict: other pages / sources of the same document or page say something else. overturned: a later pass reversed the first decision. handwritten: the page is handwritten (OCR is weaker). thin_text: the page has fewer than thin_text_chars characters of text. degraded: an optional dependency was missing and a stand-in decided.

`thin_text_chars`: 150. `calibration`: {} — Per module, optional: [[raw, calibrated], ...] knots, piecewise linear, so that a value of 0.8 means right about 8 times in 10 on labelled pages. Empty = identity. Fit from labelled pages, never by hand.

* **member**: `field_support` {"rule": 0.6, "ner": 0.4}; `note` Each identifier read off a page (name, DOB, member ID) backs its member status by its own confidence in the extraction (the staged row's model probability backed by the rules; 2026-10-26, user: not fixed numbers); without one, the rules' reading counts field_support.rule, NER's field_support.ner. The strongest is the base, each other one backs it (confidence.backed).
* **image_preprocess**: `osd_scale` 3.0; `note` Tesseract OSD's orientation confidence is open-ended (about 0-20): mapped to 0-1 as 1 - exp(-c / osd_scale), so 1 -> 0.28, 3 -> 0.63, 6 -> 0.86, 10 -> 0.96; then backed by how well the page reads as English at the chosen turn (2026-10-26).

<!-- canon-extra:end -->
